```text
   VALUE and 'the mystical' MANIFEST |  AYER gives value utterances an
   but are not factual propositions  |  EMOTIVE role - an ADDED metaethical
   (on standard readings; resolute   |  theory, not a Tractarian doctrine
   readings resist ineffable truths) |
   ==========================================================================
   THE THREE CORRECTIONS THE EXAMINER IS WATCHING FOR
   (i)   Wittgenstein was NEVER A MEMBER of the Vienna Circle
   (ii)  The TRACTATUS IS NOT A VERIFICATIONIST TEXT; the move from truth-
         conditions to verification-conditions is the CIRCLE'S OWN empiricist
         addition - and it is exactly the move that generates the strong/weak
         dilemma, since truth-conditions CAN be specified for universal laws
         while conclusive verification-conditions CANNOT
   (iii) LATER Wittgenstein no longer treats any single empirical criterion
         as the essence of meaning; his positive account is TOPIC 08 and one
         boundary sentence is sufficient here
```

⚠️ **Caption.** Rows one and two of the ledger are the answer to any "influence of Wittgenstein on logical positivism" question. Row four is the deepest of the five and is rarely written: the Tractarian base is *postulated by argument*, the positivist base is *claimed to be found* — and that is why the Circle, unlike Wittgenstein, had to hold a protocol-sentence debate at all.

#### 1. What was genuinely taken ✅

✅ Four inheritances are real and should be conceded before the correction is made.

1. **The boundary project itself** — the idea that philosophy's task is to mark the limit of what can be significantly said, rather than to say more.
2. **The status of logic** — that the propositions of logic are **tautologies**, saying nothing about the world. This is indispensable to the linguistic theory of necessary propositions (Session 8).
3. **Philosophy as an activity** — the *Tractatus*'s claim that philosophy is not a body of doctrine but a clarificatory practice, which reappears in Schlick's 1930 "turning point".
4. **The exposure of surface grammar** — the technique of showing that the apparent form of a sentence misdescribes its logical form, which Carnap turns into his diagnostic instrument (Session 6).

#### 2. What was added, and why it is not a detail ⚠️

⚠️ The addition is the substitution of **verification-conditions** for **truth-conditions**. Its three consequences:

1. **A criterion of meaning becomes a criterion of epistemic access.** Bipolarity asks whether the proposition has a determinate place in logical space; verifiability asks whether a knower can reach it.
2. **Universal laws become problematic.** Their truth-conditions are perfectly specifiable; their conclusive verification-conditions are not. ⚠️ The entire content of Session 3 follows from this single point.
3. **The base changes category.** For the *Tractatus*, simple objects are required by the conditions of determinate sense and are never exemplified; the argument is transcendental. For the Circle, the base is a class of **actual reports** — and once the base is empirical, it can be **disputed**, which is exactly what happened in 1932–35 (Session 10).

#### 3. The three fences, stated exactly ✅

✅ **Wittgenstein was never a member.** He was read, and he conversed with two members, and verificationist-sounding formulations occur in those recorded conversations. None of that is membership, and none of it makes the *Tractatus* a Circle document.

✅ **The *Tractatus* is not a verificationist text.** Its criterion of sense is **depiction**: a proposition has sense because it shares logical form with a possible situation. Whether anyone can check the situation is a further and, for the *Tractatus*, irrelevant question.

⚠️ **Later Wittgenstein is not the Circle's ally either.** He abandons the search for a single essence of meaning altogether, which pressures verificationism's uniformity but does not endorse any rival criterion. ⚠️ One sentence; the positive account is Topic 08.

#### 4. What this session earns you in an answer ⚠️

⚠️ Three marks are ordinarily available and are ordinarily lost:

| Available mark | The sentence that earns it |
|---|---|
| Historical precision | "The Circle read the *Tractatus* aloud in 1926–27 and Wittgenstein conversed with Schlick and Waismann in 1929–32, but he never joined the Circle." |
| Conceptual precision | "The move from truth-conditions to verification-conditions is the Circle's own empiricist addition." |
| Diagnostic precision | "That move is what generates the strong/weak dilemma, because truth-conditions can be specified for universal laws while conclusive verification-conditions cannot." |

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The Vienna Circle's debt to the *Tractatus* is real but must be stated precisely, because the doctrine that made logical positivism distinctive is the one thing the Circle did not take from Wittgenstein.
> **Named evidence.** The Circle read the *Tractatus* aloud proposition by proposition in 1926–27, and its influence on the 1929 manifesto is explicit; Wittgenstein conversed with Schlick and Waismann between 1929 and 1932, and verificationist formulations do occur in those conversations as recorded in *Wittgenstein and the Vienna Circle* (ed. McGuinness, 1979); yet the *Tractatus* itself grounds sense in bipolarity and truth-conditions at 4.022 and 4.024, not in any requirement that a proposition be checkable.
> **Analysis.** The Circle converted a logical requirement into an epistemic one, and the conversion is exactly what produces the school's characteristic difficulty: a universal law has perfectly determinate truth-conditions but no finite conclusive verification-conditions, so the strong sense of "verifiable" excludes the very science the criterion was meant to protect.
> **Qualification.** Wittgenstein was never a member of the Circle, the *Tractatus* is not a verificationist text, and the later Wittgenstein rejects any single criterion of meaning whatever, so his relation to the school is that of a source and then of a critic, and not that of a founder in either phase.

#### Exam link

- **2025 Q3(b)** (15 marks): one sentence of this session — that verification-conditions replaced truth-conditions — converts a competent exposition into a distinguished one, because it explains **why** the strong/weak fork exists at all.
- **2018 Q3(b)** (15 marks): §2.2 is the hinge of the second half, since the reason verification struggles with scientific sentences is the same substitution.
- **Topic 06's 2018 Q3(c) and 2021 Q4(c)** (saying and showing) are **not** owned here; §3 is the boundary that keeps a positivism answer from drifting into them, and a positivism script that ends in verificationist readings of the *Tractatus* has answered the wrong clause.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "The *Tractatus* founded logical positivism" | It was a **source**. The verification criterion is the Circle's own addition |
| "Wittgenstein was a member of the Vienna Circle" | Never. He was read in 1926–27 and conversed with Schlick and Waismann in 1929–32 |
| "Bipolarity is verifiability" | Bipolarity is **logical** — a determinate place in logical space; verifiability is **epistemic** |
| Using *unsinnig* and "cognitively meaningless" as synonyms | The *Tractatus* verdict is failure to **depict**; the positivist verdict is failure to be **testable** |
| "Later Wittgenstein returned to verificationism" | He abandons any single criterion of meaning; his account is Topic 08 |
| Explaining the picture theory at length in a positivism answer | It is Topic 06's doctrine. One clause of inheritance is the correct depth |

#### Revision notes

- Taken: the boundary project · logic as tautology · philosophy as activity · the exposure of surface grammar.
- Added: **truth-conditions → verification-conditions** — a logical requirement replaced by an epistemic one.
- Consequence: universal laws have specifiable truth-conditions but no conclusive verification-conditions → the strong/weak fork.
- Base changes category: Tractarian objects are postulated transcendentally; positivist protocols are claimed to be found — hence a protocol **debate** was possible.
- Dates: German 1921 · bilingual 1922 · Circle reading 1926–27 · conversations 1929–32 · McGuinness volume 1979.
- Three fences: Wittgenstein never a member · the *Tractatus* is not verificationist · later Wittgenstein endorses no rival criterion.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — THE LINEAGE

```text
TRACTATUS 1921 (German) / 1922 (bilingual)
   sense = TRUTH-CONDITIONS; bipolarity; logic is tautologous;
   philosophy is an activity; grammar misdescribes logical form
   |
   v
CIRCLE READS IT ALOUD 1926-27  ->  MANIFESTO 1929
CONVERSATIONS WITH SCHLICK AND WAISMANN 1929-32 (McGuinness 1979)
   |
   v
THE SUBSTITUTION:  TRUTH-CONDITIONS  ==>  VERIFICATION-CONDITIONS
   LOGICAL requirement ==> EPISTEMIC requirement
   |
   +--> universal laws: truth-conditions YES, conclusive verification NO
   |        => THE STRONG/WEAK DILEMMA                       SESSION 3
   |
   +--> base: postulated simple objects ==> claimed observation reports
            => A PROTOCOL DEBATE BECOMES POSSIBLE            SESSION 10
   |
   v
FENCES: never a member | the Tractatus is not verificationist |
   later Wittgenstein endorses no single criterion            TOPIC 08
   |
   v
ANSWER LINE: the Circle inherited a boundary and a method, and supplied the
   criterion itself - which is why the criterion's failure is the Circle's
   and not Wittgenstein's
```

### SESSION 6 — THE REJECTION OF METAPHYSICS: PSEUDO-STATEMENTS, THE GRAMMATICAL DIAGNOSIS AND "THE NOTHING NOTHINGS"

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** The positivist does not argue that the metaphysician is mistaken. He argues that the metaphysician has not managed to make a claim. The sentence has the shape of an assertion — subject, verb, object — but nothing has been asserted, because there is no way the world could be that would answer to it. The diagnosis then explains **how** intelligent people produce such sentences: they are misled by surface grammar.

**Technical definition:** A **pseudo-statement (*Scheinsatz*)** is a string that possesses the **grammatical form** of a declarative sentence without possessing the **logical function** of one: it has no truth-conditions and therefore no truth-value. ✅ Ayer: *"A metaphysical sentence is one which purports to express a genuine proposition, but does not."* Carnap's "The Elimination of Metaphysics through Logical Analysis of Language" (*Überwindung der Metaphysik durch logische Analyse der Sprache*, *Erkenntnis*, **1932**) identifies **two routes** to the defect: a constituent term for which **no empirical criterion of application** can be given, or terms that are individually meaningful but **combined so that no truth-conditions result**. ⚠️ The verdict does not deny that such strings may carry historical, poetic, emotive or practical force.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The positivist rejection of metaphysics is a diagnosis rather than a refutation: the metaphysician is not convicted of asserting something false, but of having produced a sentence that only appears to assert — and because the charge is levelled at the sentence rather than at the world, the positivist is debarred from saying that the entities in question do not exist.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **pseudo-statement (*Scheinsatz*)** — the technical verdict-word
- **surface grammar against logical form** — the source of the illusion
- **no empirical criterion of application** — route one
- **no truth-conditions from the combination** — route two
- **"the nothing nothings" (*das Nichts nichtet*)** — Heidegger, *Was ist Metaphysik?*, 1929; Carnap's paradigm
- **quantifier treated as a name** — the exact grammatical error
- **not false, not unproven, not a statement** — the strength of the verdict

**How to use them:** state the criterion **first**, then the two routes, then a worked example on each route, and then a **meaningful control case**. The control case is what distinguishes a script that has understood the test from one that has memorised a list of condemned sentences — and the 2021 part explicitly asks *how one identifies* a pseudo-statement, which is a request for a **procedure**.

#### Plain-language visual — how a sentence can look like a claim without being one

```text
   THE SHAPE OF AN ASSERTION            WHAT HAS TO BE THERE UNDERNEATH
   +-----------------------------+      +-----------------------------------+
   | SUBJECT + VERB + PREDICATE  |      | some way the world could be that  |
   | 'The table is brown'        |      | makes it TRUE                     |
   | 'The Absolute is perfect'   |      | some way it could be that makes   |
   | 'Caesar is a prime number'  |      | it FALSE                          |
   +-----------------------------+      +-----------------------------------+
        SAME SHAPE FOR ALL THREE               PRESENT ONLY FOR THE FIRST

   ROUTE 1 - A TERM WITH NO CRITERION OF APPLICATION
      'the Absolute' · 'Being-in-itself' · 'the noumenon' · 'the soul'
      No procedure exists for deciding what the term applies to
                          |
                          v
   ROUTE 2 - MEANINGFUL TERMS, ILLICITLY COMBINED
      'Caesar is a prime number'
      Every word is fine; the combination yields no truth-conditions
      'the nothing nothings' - 'nothing' is a QUANTIFIER, used as a NAME;
      'nothings' is a pseudo-verb built from it
                          |
                          v
   VERDICT: PSEUDO-STATEMENT. Not false. Not unproven. NOT A STATEMENT.
```

**In simple words:** compare three sentences with identical grammar. *The table is brown* — you know what to look at. *Caesar is a prime number* — every word is familiar, yet there is nothing to look at, because numbers and Roman generals are not the sort of things that can be predicated of one another. *The Absolute is perfect* — the subject-term has no procedure for identifying what it picks out. The three sentences fail differently, and naming the difference is what the 2021 part is asking for.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The charge is against the **sentence** | not against the object, the person or the tradition |
| Two routes, not one | dead term · illicit combination |
| Always include a **control** | a meaningful case, to prove the test discriminates |
| "Nothing" is a **quantifier** | not a name; that is the grammatical error |
| The verdict permits other uses | expressive, poetic, historical force is not denied |

> **One-line memory:** the metaphysician is not caught lying; he is caught not speaking.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the identification procedure, with graded worked examples

```text
   THE TEST, RUN AS A PROCEDURE                       [2021 Q2(b) IS THIS]
   ==========================================================================
   STEP 1  Is the sentence ANALYTIC - true or false by the meanings and
           rules of its terms alone?
              YES -> MEANINGFUL (and factually empty).  STOP.
              NO  -> go to Step 2
   STEP 2  Can any possible observation count FOR or AGAINST it?
              YES -> MEANINGFUL (synthetic).  STOP.
              NO  -> go to Step 3
   STEP 3  DIAGNOSE the failure - which route?
           ROUTE 1  a constituent TERM has no empirical criterion of
                    application
           ROUTE 2  the terms are individually significant but COMBINED so
                    that no truth-conditions result
   STEP 4  VERDICT: PSEUDO-STATEMENT (Scheinsatz).
           Record what is NOT being claimed: not false; not unproven;
           not unintelligible as an utterance; expressive or practical
           force is untouched
   ==========================================================================
   WORKED EXAMPLES - FOUR GRADES INCLUDING ONE CONTROL
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   'The soul is immortal'          PSEUDO-STATEMENT, ROUTE 1
                                   no empirical criterion for 'soul'; no
                                   observation bears on the predicate
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   'The Good transcends Being'     PSEUDO-STATEMENT, ROUTE 1 (+ ROUTE 2)
                                   neither term has a criterion, and the
                                   relation yields no truth-conditions
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   'Caesar is a prime number'      PSEUDO-STATEMENT, ROUTE 2
                                   grammatical, every word meaningful, no
                                   verification procedure applies
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   'The nothing nothings'          PSEUDO-STATEMENT, ROUTE 2 - the paradigm
   (das Nichts nichtet)            DOUBLE violation:
                                   (a) 'nothing' is a logical quantifier
                                       used as if it NAMED something
                                   (b) 'nothings' is a PSEUDO-VERB
                                       predicated of that pseudo-name
                                   Heidegger, Was ist Metaphysik?, 1929
                                   Carnap's analysis, Erkenntnis, 1932
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   'Electrons have negative        MEANINGFUL - THE CONTROL CASE
    charge'                        empirically testable, though INDIRECTLY,
                                   through a predictive theoretical network
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   '2 + 2 = 4'                     MEANINGFUL - analytic; true by the rules
                                   of arithmetic; factually empty
   ==========================================================================
   HEIDEGGER'S AVAILABLE REPLY - REPORT IT, OR THE ANSWER IS RIDICULE
   'Das Nichts' is disclosed in the mood of ANXIETY (Angst), and the
   'violation' of logical grammar is DELIBERATE, since logic itself
   presupposes the prior disclosure of beings as a whole. Reporting this is
   what converts derision into ASSESSMENT.                    See TOPIC 10
   RENDERING RULE: 'Nothing noths' or 'the nothing nothings'.
   'Nothing noughts' is NOT a rendering of this phrase and must never be
   written.
```

⚠️ **Caption.** Steps 1–4 with four graded examples and one control is the complete answer to the 2021 part. The Heidegger reply is what lifts it from a description of the doctrine to a critical discussion of it.

#### 1. The verdict, stated at its exact strength ✅

✅ Metaphysical sentences — "the Absolute is perfect", "reality is timeless", "God exists as a necessary being", "the nothing nothings" — are, on the positivist test, **cognitively meaningless**: pseudo-propositions that fail both the analytic and the empirical route.

⚠️ Three things must be said in the same breath, and each is worth a mark:

1. They are **not false**. Falsity presupposes truth-conditions.
2. They are **not merely unproven**. That description concedes that they are propositions.
3. The verdict **does not deny** that such sentences have historical, poetic, emotive or practical effects — only that they state facts.

#### 2. The diagnosis: why intelligent people produce such sentences ✅

✅ Metaphysicians are misled by **surface grammar**. The table below is the marked part of any "critically examine the rejection of metaphysics" answer, because it explains the phenomenon instead of merely condemning it.

| Surface grammar suggests… | Logical analysis reveals… |
|---|---|
| "The Absolute" behaves like a noun, so it names something | No observation could determine truth-conditions for sentences containing it; it names nothing empirical |
| "The nothing nothings" (*das Nichts nichtet*) uses "nothing" as a noun and coins a verb from it | "Nothing" is a **logical quantifier**, not a name. The sentence is parasitic on the form "Something does X", and is syntactically misleading and semantically vacuous |
| "God exists" looks like "The table exists" | For "the table exists" we know what to look for. If God is defined as transcendent, no conceivable observation bears on the sentence, so it is not false — it is meaningless |

- ⚠️ **The examinable point.** Carnap's charge against *das Nichts nichtet* is **structural**, not stylistic: a syncategorematic term has been treated as a name, and a pseudo-verb has been predicated of it. That is a demonstrable defect in logical syntax, which is precisely the advantage the Circle gained from modern logic over Hume's appeal to missing impressions.

#### 3. The two routes to pseudo-statement status, as a usable test ✅

✅ **Route 1 — a term with no empirical criterion of application.** "The Absolute", "Being-in-itself", "the noumenon", "the soul". No procedure exists for determining what such a term applies to, so no sentence containing it essentially can acquire truth-conditions.

✅ **Route 2 — meaningful terms illicitly combined.** "Caesar is a prime number" is grammatical and its words are individually significant, yet no verification procedure applies to the combination. ⚠️ *Das Nichts nichtet* belongs here in its sharpest form, because the defect is exhibited in the logical syntax itself.

⚠️ **Why the two routes must both be given.** A script that offers only route 1 cannot explain "Caesar is a prime number"; a script that offers only route 2 cannot explain "the soul is immortal", whose words are combined unexceptionably. The 2021 part asks *how one identifies* pseudo-statements, and a single route is half a procedure.

#### 4. Meaning, knowledge or the nature of things? — the 2023 architecture ⚠️

⚠️ The rejection operates **primarily at the level of meaning**, and then cascades:

| Level | What the positivist says | What he is **not** entitled to say |
|---|---|---|
| **Meaning** | The metaphysical sentence has no truth-conditions; it is a pseudo-statement | — |
| **Knowledge** | The knowledge-question is **dissolved**, not answered: one can neither know nor fail to know what is not a proposition | That we are ignorant of metaphysical truths |
| **Nature of things** | The **question** of such entities' existence is ill-formed | That the Absolute, the soul or God **does not exist** — a denial would require the very truth-conditions declared missing |

- ⚠️ **The verdict the 2023 part wants.** It is all three, **through meaning as the root**: meaning grounds knowledge, and knowledge grounds claims about the world, so a cut made at the level of meaning simultaneously removes pseudo-knowledge and pseudo-ontology. ⚠️ Saying "all of them together" without the ordering is a description; saying "all three, but meaning is the root and the other two are consequences" is an answer.

#### 5. Two refinements that keep the answer from being a slogan ⚠️

⚠️ **Carnap's later refinement.** By 1934–50 Carnap has replaced blanket dismissal with the framework apparatus: an ontological question asked **inside** a framework is legitimate and settled by its rules, while the same question asked **about** the framework is a practical proposal about which language to adopt. That is a more discriminating anti-metaphysics than Ayer's, and it does not abolish every use of ontology (Session 11).

⚠️ **The ordinary-language pressure.** Ryle- and Austin-style criticism, and the later Wittgenstein, ask whether a sentence's role can be judged apart from its ordinary circumstances of use. Commands, avowals, performatives, jokes, mathematics and religious practices may not be failed empirical reports at all. ⚠️ This does not vindicate metaphysics; it challenges the assumption that one factual-test model exhausts linguistic significance.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The logical positivist rejection of metaphysics is a diagnosis of defective sentences rather than a refutation of false doctrines, and its distinctive instrument is the logical analysis of a grammar that misleads.
> **Named evidence.** Carnap's "The Elimination of Metaphysics through Logical Analysis of Language" (*Erkenntnis*, 1932) identifies two routes to failure — a term such as "the Absolute" for which no empirical criterion of application can be given, and a combination such as "Caesar is a prime number" whose individually significant words yield no truth-conditions — and takes Heidegger's *das Nichts nichtet* ("the nothing nothings", *Was ist Metaphysik?*, 1929) as the paradigm, since it treats a logical quantifier as a name and predicates a coined verb of it; Ayer states the general verdict when he writes that a metaphysical sentence "purports to express a genuine proposition, but does not".
> **Analysis.** Because the charge is levelled at the sentence, its consequences run downward rather than outward: the question of knowledge is dissolved rather than answered, since one can neither know nor fail to know what is not a proposition, and the ontological question is declared ill-formed rather than settled, since a denial of the Absolute's existence would require precisely the truth-conditions the positivist says are absent.
> **Qualification.** The diagnosis is only as strong as the criterion it applies, and it faces two live replies: Heidegger holds that the disclosure of *das Nichts* in anxiety precedes logic and that the breach of logical grammar is deliberate, while ordinary-language critics deny that a single factual-test model exhausts significance — so the honest verdict is that positivism showed how metaphysical sentences are generated without showing that no such sentence could ever be significant.

#### Exam link

- **2021 Q2(b)** (15 marks) is §1, §3 and the procedure visual, with the control case and Heidegger's reply supplying the "critically" half.
- **2023 Q3(a)** (20 marks) is §4's three-level table, closed by the "all three, through meaning as the root" verdict and assessed with §5.
- **2018 Q3(b)** (15 marks) takes its first half from §1 and §2 — *how* the positivists show metaphysical sentences to be meaningless.
- **2020 Q1(e)** (10 marks) uses route 1 on "the Soul of Superman" before Session 7 supplies the value-predicate half.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Positivists reject metaphysics as **false**" | As **cognitively meaningless**. Falsity presupposes the truth-conditions they say are absent |
| "Positivists deny that God or the soul exists" | They declare the **question** ill-formed. A denial would be a meaningful claim needing the missing truth-conditions |
| Writing **"Nothing noughts"** | ❌ Never. Correct renderings: **"Nothing noths"** or **"the nothing nothings"** for *das Nichts nichtet* |
| Attributing *das Nichts nichtet* to Carnap | It is **Heidegger's** (*Was ist Metaphysik?*, 1929); **Carnap** analyses it in 1932 |
| Ridiculing Heidegger without reporting his reply | The reply — *Angst* discloses *das Nichts*, and logic presupposes that disclosure — is what makes the answer critical |
| Giving one route only | Two routes are needed: **no criterion of application** and **illicit combination** |
| Listing condemned sentences with no control case | Include a meaningful case such as "electrons have negative charge" to show the test discriminates |

#### Revision notes

- Verdict: pseudo-statement (*Scheinsatz*) — grammatical form of an assertion without its logical function; **no truth-conditions, no truth-value**.
- Ayer: a metaphysical sentence "purports to express a genuine proposition, but does not".
- Carnap 1932, *Erkenntnis*: "The Elimination of Metaphysics through Logical Analysis of Language".
- Two routes: (i) term with no empirical criterion of application; (ii) significant terms combined without truth-conditions.
- Paradigm: *das Nichts nichtet* (Heidegger, 1929) — quantifier used as a name **plus** a pseudo-verb. Never "Nothing noughts".
- Heidegger's reply: *das Nichts* is disclosed in anxiety (*Angst*); the breach of logical grammar is deliberate.
- 2023 architecture: meaning is primary; knowledge is **dissolved**; ontology is declared **ill-formed** — all three, through meaning as the root.
- Refinements: Carnap's internal/external distinction (Session 11); ordinary-language pressure on the one-model assumption.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — THE DIAGNOSIS

```text
ASSERTION-SHAPED SENTENCE
   |
   +--> ANALYTIC?          YES -> meaningful, factually empty.  STOP
   +--> ANY OBSERVATION
        RELEVANT?          YES -> meaningful, synthetic.        STOP
   |
   +--> NO TO BOTH -> DIAGNOSE THE ROUTE
           ROUTE 1  term with NO empirical criterion of application
                    'the Absolute' · 'the soul' · 'the noumenon'
           ROUTE 2  significant terms, NO truth-conditions from the
                    combination: 'Caesar is a prime number';
                    'das Nichts nichtet' - quantifier as name + pseudo-verb
   |
   v
VERDICT: PSEUDO-STATEMENT (Scheinsatz)
   NOT false · NOT unproven · NOT a denial of existence
   expressive / poetic / practical force UNTOUCHED
   |
   v
CASCADE (2023):  MEANING is the root
   -> KNOWLEDGE dissolved (no proposition to know or fail to know)
   -> ONTOLOGY declared ill-formed (not: 'these things do not exist')
   |
   v
REPLIES: Heidegger (Angst discloses das Nichts; the breach is deliberate) |
   ordinary-language (one factual-test model does not exhaust significance)
   |
   v
ANSWER LINE: the metaphysician is not caught lying; he is caught not
   speaking - and the charge is only as strong as the criterion behind it
```

### SESSION 7 — ETHICS, AESTHETICS AND THEOLOGY: EMOTIVISM, AYER AGAINST STEVENSON, AND THE VALUE PREDICATE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** If a sentence counts as fact-stating only when it is analytic or testable, then "stealing is wrong" is in trouble: it is not true by definition, and no measurement settles it. Ayer's answer is that such sentences were never in the business of stating facts at all. They **express** an attitude, and may **arouse** one, much as a tone of voice does. Stevenson agrees that they are not reports, but adds that they carry descriptive content too, and — crucially — that moral argument is therefore still possible.

**Technical definition:** **Emotivism** is the non-cognitivist thesis that moral utterances do not state propositions but express attitudes. For **Ayer** (*Language, Truth and Logic*, 1936, ch. 6) it is a **corollary of the verification principle**: ethical sentences are neither analytic nor empirically verifiable, hence non-cognitive; adding "…is wrong" to "you stole that money" adds **no factual content whatever** but *evinces* disapproval and may *evoke* it. For **C. L. Stevenson** ("The Emotive Meaning of Ethical Terms", *Mind*, **1937**; *Ethics and Language*, **1944**) ethical terms possess **both** a **descriptive** and an **emotive** meaning, and the theory is built bottom-up from the psychology of linguistic influence rather than derived from any criterion of factual meaning. ⚠️ Neither is a **subjectivist**: both deny that "X is wrong" **reports** the speaker's feeling.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Emotivism is best assessed by refusing to treat it as one doctrine: Ayer's version is a deduction from the verification principle and shares its fate, whereas Stevenson's is an independently motivated account of how language redirects attitude, and it explains the one thing Ayer could not — how moral argument can be genuine even though moral judgements state no fact.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **non-cognitivism, not subjectivism** — expression is not report; the single most valuable discrimination here
- **evince and evoke** — Ayer's two verbs
- **descriptive meaning and emotive meaning** — Stevenson's two-part account
- **disagreement in belief against disagreement in attitude** — Stevenson's crucial innovation
- **persuasive definition** — Stevenson's distinctive further doctrine
- **Frege–Geach embedding problem** — the decisive objection; Geach 1960, 1965, anticipated by Frege
- **cognitively meaningless, not useless** — the boundary sentence for the 2020 part

**How to use them:** never write "emotivism (Ayer, Stevenson)" as a single label. Split them in the first sentence, and the answer is already above average. Then rule: the doctrine survives only in Stevenson's form, and even then only until Frege–Geach is met.

#### Plain-language visual — three different things "stealing is wrong" might be doing

```text
   OPTION 1  SUBJECTIVISM (both Ayer and Stevenson REJECT this)
   'Stealing is wrong'  =  'I disapprove of stealing'
        a REPORT about the speaker; TRUE OR FALSE
        => moral disagreement becomes disagreement about autobiography,
           and two people could never contradict one another
                          |  REJECTED
                          v
   OPTION 2  AYER'S EMOTIVISM
   'You stole that money'          <- states the whole fact
   'You stole that money - WRONG!' <- adds NO factual content
        the added element EVINCES the speaker's disapproval
        and may EVOKE it in the hearer.  It is an EXPRESSION,
        not a report. NO propositional paraphrase exists.
                          |
                          v
   OPTION 3  STEVENSON'S EMOTIVISM
   'X is good'  ~  'I approve of X; DO SO AS WELL.'
        an expression of attitude PLUS an imperative / persuasive element,
        and the term ALSO carries a DESCRIPTIVE meaning that varies with
        context
                          |
                          v
   WHY OPTION 3 MATTERS: only it leaves room for moral ARGUMENT
```

**In simple words:** the difference between reporting and expressing is the difference between "I am in pain" and "Ouch!". The first can be false — I might be lying. The second cannot be false; it is not that kind of thing. Ayer says moral language belongs with "Ouch!". Stevenson agrees that it is not a report, but observes that people **do** argue about morality and give reasons — and builds a theory that explains how, without turning moral sentences back into statements of fact.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Both are **non-cognitivists** | neither says "X is wrong" reports a feeling |
| Ayer: **pure expression** | no propositional paraphrase at all |
| Stevenson: **descriptive + emotive** | two kinds of meaning, not one |
| Ayer: ethical disputes are **really about facts** | once facts are agreed, "we should not attempt to argue" |
| Stevenson: **disagreement in attitude** | survives complete factual agreement |
| The killer objection is **embedding** | Frege–Geach |

> **One-line memory:** Ayer explains why moral sentences state no fact; Stevenson explains why we argue about them anyway.

⚠️ **Caption.** The last three rows are the assessment. A script that gives the comparison but not the objections has described two theories; a script that adds Frege–Geach and rules has evaluated them.

#### 1. Why ethics arrives in this clause at all — the ownership note ⚠️

⚠️ Ethics is not a printed limb of item 7. It enters for two precise reasons, and the answer should say so:

1. **Ayer applies the cognitive criterion to value language**, so emotivism is a **consequence** of the verification principle and belongs to any complete account of the principle's reach.
2. **The 2020 part asks about "Good"**, which forces the distinction between expressive and factual significance.

- ❌ It follows that a full survey of twentieth-century metaethics does not belong here. Blackburn and Gibbard are named once, as the later expressivists who attempted what Ayer and Stevenson could not, and no further.

#### 2. Theology under the criterion, at owned depth ✅

✅ Transcendent existence-claims fail Ayer's factual criterion by exactly the route of Session 6: if God is defined as beyond possible experience, then no observation is relevant, and "God exists" is a pseudo-statement rather than a false hypothesis.

⚠️ Three qualifications must accompany it, because the unqualified verdict is where scripts overreach:

1. The verdict is **not atheism**. Atheism is a meaningful denial; the positivist declines the question. ⚠️ Ayer himself distinguishes his position from atheism for this reason.
2. **Ritual, expressive, communal and practical religious uses** are a separate question, not thereby analysed exhaustively.
3. The theological reply — that religious utterance might be verifiable **after death**, which is John Hick's **eschatological verification** — is a genuine response and is **owned by Paper II, Religious Language**. ⚠️ Name it in one clause as the cross-paper route and stop.

#### 3. "The Soul of Superman is Good" — the 2020 part, decomposed ✅

✅ The sentence is grammatically declarative but must be split into **two independent failures**, and the marks are in the split:

| Element | Analysis | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **"The Soul of Superman"** — the subject | No observation-procedure is specified for identifying a "soul", and the bearer is a **fictional** character, so no empirical criterion of application is available | Route 1 pseudo-statement material (Session 6) |
| **"is Good"** — the predicate | On Ayer's account the value term adds **no factual content**; it evinces approval and may evoke it | Non-cognitive: expressive, not descriptive |
| **The whole sentence** | Neither analytic nor empirically verifiable | ✅ **Cognitively meaningless — not false** |

⚠️ **The qualification that completes the answer.** ❌ Do not claim that every fictional or ethical sentence is meaningless in every use. Within a **fiction-framework** — "in the story, Superman is portrayed as good" — the sentence becomes a checkable claim about a text. The question concerns **factual cognitive meaning under the theory**, and saying so demonstrates exactly the control a ten-mark part rewards in its final line.

#### 4. The executable verdict ⚠️

⚠️ **Write this, adapted:** Ayer's emotivism is a **deduction** from the verification principle and shares its fate; Stevenson's is an **independent theory of linguistic influence** which explains what Ayer could not — how moral argument can be genuine even though moral judgements state no fact. If asked to assess emotivism, distinguish the two and rule that the doctrine survives only in Stevenson's form, and even then only until the Frege–Geach problem is met.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Emotivism must be split before it can be assessed, because Ayer's version stands or falls with the verification principle while Stevenson's does not depend on it at all.
> **Named evidence.** Ayer, in *Language, Truth and Logic* (1936, ch. 6), holds that adding "…is wrong" to "you stole that money" contributes no factual content and merely evinces disapproval, and he concludes that once the surrounding facts are agreed there is nothing left to argue about; Stevenson, in "The Emotive Meaning of Ethical Terms" (*Mind*, 1937) and *Ethics and Language* (1944), gives ethical terms a descriptive as well as an emotive meaning, models "X is good" as "I approve of X; do so as well", and separates disagreement in belief from disagreement in attitude.
> **Analysis.** The separation matters because it decides whether moral reasoning is possible: on Ayer's account reasons operate only on the factual surround, whereas on Stevenson's they work causally on the beliefs that sustain an attitude, which is why he can distinguish rational from merely persuasive methods and can name persuasive definition as a recognisable abuse.
> **Qualification.** Both remain non-cognitivists rather than subjectivists, since neither treats a moral sentence as a report of the speaker's feeling; and both are exposed by the Frege–Geach embedding problem, since the antecedent of "if lying is wrong, then getting your brother to lie is wrong" expresses no attitude yet must retain the same meaning for the inference to be valid — a difficulty neither answered, and which later expressivists such as Blackburn and Gibbard attempted to meet with logics of attitude.

#### Exam link

- **2020 Q1(e)** (10 marks) is §3, opened by the criterion and closed by the fiction-framework qualification.
- **2023 Q3(a)** (20 marks) uses §2 to show the **cascade** into a further domain: theology is refused at the level of meaning, which is why the positivist verdict is not atheism.
- **2021 Q2(b)** (15 marks) can use the value-sentence as an additional graded example, distinguishing it from "the Absolute is perfect" — the first has a non-descriptive **use**, the second does not.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Emotivism (Ayer, Stevenson)" as one doctrine | Two doctrines with different foundations and opposite verdicts on whether moral argument is possible |
| "Emotivism says 'X is wrong' means 'I disapprove of X'" | That is **subjectivism**, which **both** reject; expression is not report |
| "Emotivism says moral judgements are meaningless" | Cognitively meaningless — they state no fact — but they have emotive and prescriptive **force** |
| "Positivism entails atheism" | It declines the question. Atheism is a **meaningful denial** requiring the missing truth-conditions |
| Developing Hick's eschatological verification | One clause; it is owned by **Paper II, Religious Language** |
| Claiming every fictional sentence is meaningless | Within an explicit fiction-framework the sentence becomes checkable; the verdict concerns **factual** cognitive meaning |
| Omitting Frege–Geach in an "assess emotivism" answer | It is the decisive objection and neither Ayer nor Stevenson answered it |

#### Revision notes

- Ayer (*LTL* 1936, ch. 6): value terms **evince** and may **evoke**; no factual content added; emotivism is a **corollary** of the criterion.
- Stevenson (*Mind* 1937; *Ethics and Language* 1944): **descriptive + emotive** meaning; "I approve of X; do so as well"; **persuasive definition**.
- Both are **non-cognitivists**, not subjectivists — expression ≠ report.
- Ayer: disputes are really factual; once facts are agreed, argument stops. Stevenson: **disagreement in attitude** survives factual agreement; rational vs persuasive methods.
- Frege–Geach (Geach 1960, 1965; anticipated by Frege): unasserted contexts; decisive against simple emotivism; Blackburn and Gibbard later attempt logics of attitude.
- Theology: transcendent claims fail the criterion; **not atheism**; ritual/expressive uses untouched; Hick's eschatological verification is Paper II.
- 2020 split: fictional subject-term with no criterion of application **+** non-descriptive value predicate = cognitively meaningless, not false, and not meaningless within a stated fiction-framework.

```text
   |
   +--> analytic?  NO      +--> empirically verifiable?  NO
   |
   v
NON-COGNITIVE: states no fact
   |
   +--> AYER 1936  PURE EXPRESSION - evince / evoke; no paraphrase;
   |      corollary of the VERIFICATION PRINCIPLE (top-down)
   |      => moral argument only about the surrounding FACTS
   |
   +--> STEVENSON 1937/1944  DESCRIPTIVE + EMOTIVE meaning;
          'I approve of X; do so as well'; PERSUASIVE DEFINITION;
          independent of the criterion (bottom-up)
          => DISAGREEMENT IN ATTITUDE survives factual agreement
          => rational vs non-rational methods; argument IS possible
   |
   v

   |

   Decisive against simple emotivism; unanswered by both

   expressive/ritual uses untouched; Hick's eschatological verification
   = PAPER II

   v

   predicate  =>  cognitively meaningless, NOT false
   |
   v
ANSWER LINE: Ayer explains why moral sentences state no fact; Stevenson
   explains why we argue about them anyway - and Frege-Geach explains why
   neither account is finished
```

### SESSION 8 — THE LINGUISTIC THEORY OF NECESSARY PROPOSITIONS: THE TRILEMMA, TAUTOLOGY AND THE EXHAUSTIVE DICHOTOMY

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** An empiricist has a problem with mathematics. If all knowledge comes from experience, how can we be certain that 2 + 2 = 4 without checking every case — and why does no possible experience count against it? The positivist answer is disarming: because the sentence says nothing about the world. Its certainty is bought by its emptiness. It records how we have decided to use our symbols, and that is why nothing that happens can disturb it.

**Technical definition:** The **linguistic theory of necessary propositions** — the third printed limb of the clause — holds that necessary propositions, comprising logic, mathematics and definitions, are **analytic**: true solely in virtue of the meanings of, and the conventions governing, their symbols. They are **tautologies**; they give **no information about the world**; their necessity is **verbal** or **conventional**. ⚠️ The theory's polemical point is that the **analytic/synthetic classification is exhaustive**: there is no third box, and Kant's **synthetic a priori** is therefore declared incoherent.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The linguistic theory of necessary propositions is not primarily a theory of mathematics but an escape route for empiricism: by making necessity verbal, the positivists purchased the certainty of logic and mathematics at the price of their factual content, and so dissolved the synthetic a priori that Kant had made the centre of his system.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **necessary, a priori, certain** — the three properties that create the problem
- **analytic** — true solely in virtue of the meanings and rules governing the symbols
- **tautology** — the form of the necessary proposition; P ∨ ¬P
- **true by convention** — the account of *why* it is necessary
- **empty of factual content** — the price paid, and the whole point
- **exhaustive dichotomy** — there is no third box
- **synthetic a priori is a myth** — Kant's category dissolved
- **P ∨ ¬P** — write the form; it converts assertion into demonstration

**How to use them:** state the **trilemma first** — necessity plus apriority plus certainty against "all knowledge comes from experience" — and only then give the answer. A script that defines "analytic" and stops has answered a definition question inside an elucidation question, which is the single commonest way this limb is under-marked.

#### Plain-language visual — the problem, and the three ways out

```text
   THE EMPIRICIST'S PROBLEM
   +--------------------------------------------------------------+
   | LOGIC AND MATHEMATICS ARE:                                    |
   |   NECESSARY   - they cannot be otherwise                      |
   |   A PRIORI    - not learned from experience                   |
   |   CERTAIN     - not defeasible                                |
   | YET EMPIRICISM SAYS: ALL KNOWLEDGE COMES FROM EXPERIENCE      |
   +--------------------------------------------------------------+
                                |
        +-----------------------+------------------------+
        v                       v                        v
   KANT                    MILL                     POSITIVISM
   they ARE synthetic      they are just very       they are ANALYTIC -
   - they say something    well-confirmed           true by the MEANINGS
   about the world -       EMPIRICAL                and CONVENTIONS of the
   but known a priori      generalisations          symbols. They say
   because the MIND        about collections        NOTHING about the world.
   IMPOSES structure       of objects               Their necessity is VERBAL
        |                       |                        |
        v                       v                        v
   EMPIRICISTS REJECT      ABSURD: we do not        THE PRICE: certainty is
   a 'third box'           DISCOVER 2+2=4 by        bought by EMPTINESS -
   beyond analytic and     counting pebbles,        and that is exactly what
   synthetic               and no experience        the positivist wants
                           could REFUTE it
```

**In simple words:** why can no experiment refute "all bachelors are unmarried"? Not because the sentence is protected by some special faculty, but because it does not forbid anything. It rules out no possible observation, so no observation can conflict with it. The same, says the positivist, holds for the whole of logic and mathematics. Their invulnerability is the invulnerability of a rule, not the invulnerability of a super-fact.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The problem is a **trilemma** | necessary + a priori + certain vs all knowledge from experience |
| Three rivals | Kant (synthetic a priori) · Mill (empirical generalisation) · positivism (analytic) |
| The answer trades **content for certainty** | empty, therefore unrefutable |
| The dichotomy is **exhaustive** | no third box; Kant's category dissolved |
| Mill is refuted by a **thought-experiment** | no counting could refute 2 + 2 = 4 |

> **One-line memory:** a necessary proposition is certain because it forbids nothing.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the three rivals, the four features, and the two-basket grid

```text
                                     Its truth depends only on the meanings

     cannot be non-circularly defined, and 'truth by convention' cannot

     without already using logic                             TOPIC 11
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The 2024 pair sits inside this table already worked. Note the last block: a script that presents the linguistic theory without naming a single pressure on it has described a doctrine rather than elucidated one.

#### 1. The trilemma, stated so that the solution has something to solve ✅

✅ Logic and mathematics are **necessary** (they cannot be otherwise), **known a priori** (not learned from experience) and **certain** (not defeasible). ⚠️ Empiricism holds that all knowledge derives from experience. The three properties and the empiricist thesis cannot all be true unless necessary truths are of a special kind — and specifying that kind is the third printed limb of the syllabus.

#### 2. The answer, and the exact nature of the trade ✅

✅ Necessary propositions are **analytic**: true by virtue of the meanings of the terms used, that is, by **linguistic convention**. They are **tautologies** and give **no information about the world**.

⚠️ The trade must be made explicit, because it is the doctrine's whole content: **certainty is purchased with emptiness**. "All bachelors are unmarried" cannot be refuted because it rules out nothing; it records a resolve to use two expressions interchangeably. ⚠️ The same holds, on this account, for the whole of logic and arithmetic.

#### 3. Why Mill fails, and why the failure matters ✅

✅ Mill treats "2 + 2 = 4" as a very well-confirmed generalisation about collections. ⚠️ Two objections are decisive and are worth one sentence each: we do not **discover** the truth by counting pebbles, and **no experience could refute it** — if two pairs of objects failed to make four, we would say something had been miscounted or that an object had been created or destroyed, not that arithmetic had been falsified.

- ⚠️ **Why this matters for the answer.** Mill is the **empiricist** rival, so refuting him shows that the positivist account is not merely one empiricism among others but the only empiricism that keeps mathematics intact. That is the strongest positive case available for the third limb.

#### 4. The exhaustive dichotomy and the death of the synthetic a priori ✅

✅ The classification is **exhaustive**: every cognitively meaningful proposition is analytic or synthetic, and the category **synthetic a priori** is declared incoherent. ⚠️ Kant's central innovation — that mathematics and pure natural science furnish judgements which are both informative and necessary — is thereby dissolved rather than refuted in Kant's own terms.

⚠️ Two boundary notes:

1. This is the **exact opposite** of Kant's position, and the confusion of the two is one of the most heavily penalised errors in this clause. The positivists **deny** the synthetic a priori; they say necessity is **analytic**, never synthetic.
2. Kant's positive machinery — the Copernican turn, the categories, the transcendental deduction — is **Topic 04**. Here he appears **only as the target**.

#### 5. The pressures, stated honestly ⚠️

⚠️ **Quine.** "Truth by Convention" (1936) presses an early version of the difficulty: conventions must be **applied**, and applying them to derive their infinitely many consequences already presupposes logic, so logic cannot be **explained** as true by convention without circularity. "Two Dogmas of Empiricism" (1951) then attacks the analytic/synthetic distinction itself. ⚠️ If Quine succeeds, the linguistic theory rests on a category that is itself unclear. The full dispute, and the Carnapian replies, belong to **Topic 11**.

⚠️ **Applicability.** If mathematics is factually empty, its indispensability to physics needs explanation. The available positivist reply is that applicability belongs to the **empirical theory** in which the mathematics is embedded: the mathematics supplies the transformation rules, and the empirical content is contributed by the theory's contact with observation.

❓ **Gödel.** Limitative results constrain **particular** formal systems and their capacity to prove their own consistency. ❌ They are not a one-line refutation of every conventionalist account of necessity, and writing them as such is an overreach the examiner will notice.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The linguistic theory of necessary propositions is empiricism's solution to a problem of its own making: it preserves the necessity, apriority and certainty of logic and mathematics by denying that they say anything at all.
> **Named evidence.** The positivists classify "all bachelors are unmarried" and "all objects are either red or not red" — the latter of the form P ∨ ¬P — as analytic, true solely in virtue of the meanings of their terms and of the logical constants, and therefore as tautologies that no observation could refute; they reject Mill's account of "2 + 2 = 4" as an empirical generalisation, on the ground that no counting could refute it; and they declare the analytic/synthetic classification exhaustive, so that Kant's synthetic a priori is not refuted but dissolved.
> **Analysis.** The account delivers all three demanded properties from a single source, since a proposition whose truth follows from the rules governing its symbols is necessary because nothing could count against it, a priori because grasping the meanings suffices, and certain because its denial is self-contradictory; and the cost — total absence of factual content — is not an embarrassment but the mechanism by which the properties are secured.
> **Qualification.** The account is nevertheless exposed at two points: Quine argues in "Truth by Convention" (1936) that conventions cannot generate their own consequences without presupposing the logic they are meant to explain, and in "Two Dogmas of Empiricism" (1951) that analyticity cannot be non-circularly defined; and the indispensability of mathematics in physics still requires the positivist to locate applicability in the embedding empirical theory rather than in the mathematics itself.

#### Exam link

- **2024 Q3(b)** (15 marks) is this session's worked pair: the answer is **no**, both meaningful, but by different branches, with the tautology's emptiness stated explicitly.
- **2023 Q3(a)** (20 marks) uses §4: the denial of the synthetic a priori is what forces metaphysics into the pseudo-statement class, since metaphysics is precisely the pretension to synthetic a priori knowledge of reality.
- **2025 Q3(b)** (15 marks) needs the analytic branch in the exposition; the criterion's **first disjunct** is unintelligible without it.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "The linguistic theory = Kant's synthetic a priori" | **Exactly opposite.** The positivists **deny** the synthetic a priori; necessity is analytic, never synthetic |
| "'All objects are either red or not red' is empirical" | It is a **tautology**, P ∨ ¬P — analytic, factually empty, meaningful as a **logical** truth |
| "Analytic sentences are meaningless because they say nothing" | They are **meaningful and empty**. They pass by the **first** branch of the criterion |
| Defining "analytic" and stopping | State the **trilemma** first, so that the theory answers a question |
| Ignoring Mill | Mill is the empiricist rival; refuting him is the strongest positive case for the doctrine |
| "Gödel refuted the linguistic theory" | Gödel constrains **particular formal systems**; he is not a one-line refutation of conventionalism |
| Developing "Two Dogmas" at length | One or two clauses. The dispute is **Topic 11** |

#### Revision notes

- Trilemma: necessary + a priori + certain **against** all knowledge from experience.
- Three rivals: Kant (synthetic a priori) · Mill (empirical generalisation) · positivism (analytic/conventional).
- Positivist answer: necessary truths are **tautologies**, true by the meanings and conventions of the symbols; **empty of factual content**.
- Four features from one source: necessary (nothing counts against) · a priori (meanings suffice) · certain (denial self-contradictory) · empty (records a convention).
- Mill refuted: we do not discover 2 + 2 = 4 by counting; no experience could refute it.
- Exhaustive dichotomy: **no third box**; the synthetic a priori is declared incoherent; Kant appears only as target (Topic 04).
- 2024 pair: tautology P ∨ ¬P (analytic, empty) against "this page is white" (synthetic, checkable) — **both meaningful, different branches**.
- Pressures: Quine 1936 and 1951 (Topic 11) · applicability of mathematics · Gödel bounded, not decisive.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — NECESSITY MADE VERBAL

```text
PROBLEM: logic and maths are NECESSARY + A PRIORI + CERTAIN,
   yet empiricism says ALL KNOWLEDGE COMES FROM EXPERIENCE
   |
   +--> KANT: they are SYNTHETIC A PRIORI  -> empiricists reject a third box
   +--> MILL: empirical generalisations    -> absurd; no experience refutes
   +--> POSITIVISM: they are ANALYTIC      -> true by MEANING and CONVENTION
   |
   v
TAUTOLOGY: 'All bachelors are unmarried' · '2 + 2 = 4' · P v ~P
   NECESSARY  nothing could count against them
   A PRIORI   grasping the meanings suffices
   CERTAIN    denial is self-contradiction
   EMPTY      they record conventions, not facts     <- THE PRICE AND POINT
   |
   v
EXHAUSTIVE DICHOTOMY: ANALYTIC | SYNTHETIC - NO THIRD BOX
   => SYNTHETIC A PRIORI DISSOLVED (Kant = target only, TOPIC 04)
   => metaphysics, which claims synthetic a priori knowledge of reality,
      falls into the PSEUDO-STATEMENT class                    SESSION 6
   |
   v
2024: 'All objects are either red or not red' (ANALYTIC, empty)
      'This page is white' (SYNTHETIC, checkable)
      BOTH meaningful - DIFFERENT BRANCHES.  Answer: NO
   |
   v
PRESSURES: Quine 1936 conventions presuppose logic | Quine 1951 analyticity
   undefined (TOPIC 11) | applicability of maths | Godel bounded
   |
   v
ANSWER LINE: a necessary proposition is certain because it forbids nothing
```

### SESSION 9 — GENERAL STATEMENTS, SCIENTIFIC LAWS AND THEORETICAL TERMS: FROM VERIFICATION TO CONFIRMATION

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** A law of nature is a claim about every case, everywhere, always. No finite run of experiments can finish confirming it. If a criterion of meaning demands conclusive verification, the laws of physics are condemned along with metaphysics — which is plainly the wrong result. So the positivist must show how a general statement can be answerable to experience **without** being conclusively verifiable, and then show that a metaphysical universal cannot be answerable in the same way.

**Technical definition:** A **universal empirical law** ("all metals expand when heated") is **synthetic** but not conclusively verifiable by finitely many observations. Ayer's **weak** route treats relevant observations and successful consequences as rendering such a statement **probable**; later **confirmation theory** develops **degrees of evidential support**. A **theoretical term** ("electron") acquires empirical significance not by one-to-one correspondence with an observation but through its role in **laws, auxiliary assumptions and testable consequences of a theory-network**. ⚠️ This movement from verification toward confirmation and system-level testing repairs the account of science but **loosens** the original sharp sentence-by-sentence meaning criterion. ❌ **Potential falsification is Popper's contrasting demarcation proposal, not Ayer's definition of meaning.**

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The verification theory's hardest test is not metaphysics but physics: a law of nature is a claim about indefinitely many unobserved cases, so the positivist must find a way for a sentence to be answerable to experience without ever being settled by it — and the account he reaches, of indirect and system-level confirmation, saves science precisely by weakening the boundary he built the criterion to draw.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **universal empirical law** — the hard case, named
- **not conclusively verifiable by finitely many observations** — the exact difficulty
- **testable consequences** — how the law reaches experience
- **indirect and system-dependent** — the character of the connection
- **degrees of confirmation** — what replaces verification
- **theory-network** — how "electron" gets its content
- **no comparable test** — the reason metaphysical universals are still excluded

**How to use them:** the 2018 and 2019 parts both have a two-part structure — *here is the account*, then *can it be extended?* Answer the extension question with a **reason**, not a verdict: general scientific statements have **systematic empirical consequences**, and metaphysical universals specify **no comparable test**. That sentence is the pivot of both answers.

#### Plain-language visual — how a law touches the world without ever being finished

```text
   'ALL METALS EXPAND WHEN HEATED'
              |
              | can it be CONCLUSIVELY verified?
              v
   +-----------------------------------------------+
   | NO. Indefinitely many instances; a finite      |
   | observation set never exhausts them            |
   +-----------------------------------------------+
              |
              | then how does it touch experience AT ALL?
              v
   LAW  +  INITIAL CONDITIONS  +  AUXILIARY ASSUMPTIONS
              |
              v
          PREDICTION       'this copper bar, heated, will lengthen'
              |
              v
        OBSERVATION REPORT
              |
      +-------+---------+
      |                 |
   MATCHES           FAILS
      |                 |
      v                 v
   the SYSTEM is     something in the SYSTEM must be revised -
   CONFIRMED         and the law is only ONE candidate for revision
   (supported,
    never proved)
              |
              v
   NOW APPLY THE SAME MACHINE TO 'ALL REALITY IS SPIRITUAL'
              |
              v
   NO INITIAL CONDITIONS, NO AUXILIARIES, NO PREDICTION,
   NOTHING TO OBSERVE - THE MACHINE WILL NOT START
```

**In simple words:** a scientific law is like a promissory note that can be cashed indefinitely often but never fully redeemed. Every successful prediction is a partial payment. A metaphysical universal is not a note at all: there is no counter at which to present it. That contrast — **partially cashable** against **not cashable at all** — is the exact answer to the second half of both the 2018 and the 2019 parts.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Laws are **synthetic** | contingent, factual — never analytic |
| They are **not conclusively** verifiable | finitely many observations never exhaust them |
| They reach experience **indirectly** | through predictions from law + conditions + auxiliaries |
| Confirmation comes in **degrees** | support, not proof |
| Failure implicates the **system** | the law is only one candidate for revision |
| Metaphysical universals have **no test at all** | the difference is categorical, not one of degree |

> **One-line memory:** a law can be paid in instalments; a metaphysical universal presents no bill.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the four hard cases, each with its route and its residual cost

```text
   THE FOUR CLASSES THAT STRAIN THE CRITERION
   ==========================================================================
   1  UNIVERSAL LAWS  'All metals expand when heated'
      DIFFICULTY   indefinitely many instances; strong verification fails
      ROUTE        law + initial conditions + auxiliaries -> prediction ->
                   observation report -> the SYSTEM is confirmed or strained
      LATER        confirmation theory supplies DEGREES of support
      COST         support is never proof; and failure never points at ONE
                   sentence, which is where holism later enters   TOPIC 11
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   2  STATEMENTS ABOUT THE PAST  'Caesar crossed the Rubicon'
      DIFFICULTY   the event is not now observable
      ROUTE        present RECORDS, TRACES and TESTIMONY, assessed through
                   causal and historical theory
      COST         a strict phenomenalist reduction threatens to CONFUSE the
                   EVIDENCE for the claim with its PAST-DIRECTED CONTENT -
                   the sentence is about Caesar, not about the parchment
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   3  OTHER MINDS  'She is in pain'
      DIFFICULTY   another's experience is not an object of my observation
      ROUTE        publicly observable BEHAVIOUR, LANGUAGE and explanatory
                   THEORY provide indirect, non-conclusive support
      COST         reducing pain SIMPLY to behaviour is ONE behaviourist
                   route, NOT the only possible weak-verification account -
                   do not present it as forced
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   4  THEORETICAL ENTITIES  'Electrons have negative charge'
      DIFFICULTY   no single observation corresponds to the term
      ROUTE        significance through the term's ROLE in laws, auxiliary
                   assumptions and the testable consequences of a
                   THEORY-NETWORK
      COST         if a term gets its content from a network, then meaning
                   is NOT assigned sentence by sentence - and the criterion
                   was a SENTENCE-BY-SENTENCE criterion
   ==========================================================================
   THE MOVEMENT THIS FORCES
   VERIFICATION  ->  WEAK / INDIRECT VERIFICATION  ->  CONFIRMATION AND
   TESTABILITY OF SYSTEMS
      GAIN  a far better account of how evidence supports scientific theory
      LOSS  the sharp, sentence-by-sentence boundary between the meaningful
            and the meaningless
   ==========================================================================
   WHY METAPHYSICAL UNIVERSALS ARE STILL EXCLUDED   [2019 Q2(a), second half]
   'All reality is spiritual' specifies NO observation that would count for
   or against it, NOT EVEN IN PRINCIPLE. It generates no prediction under any
   initial conditions and with any auxiliaries. General SCIENTIFIC statements
   have SYSTEMATIC EMPIRICAL CONSEQUENCES and remain open to confirming and
   disconfirming evidence; metaphysical universals specify NO COMPARABLE
   TEST.  The difference is CATEGORICAL, not a difference of degree.
   ==========================================================================
   THE HONEST RESIDUE   [2018 Q3(b), second half]
   The routes above are INDIRECT and THEORY-DEPENDENT. That weakens simple
   sentence-by-sentence reduction WITHOUT making every such statement
   meaningless. So the answer to 'can verification account for the
   meaningfulness of ALL scientific sentences?' is: NOT ON THE STRONG SENSE;
   ON THE WEAK SENSE ONLY AT THE COST OF THE SHARP BOUNDARY.
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The two routed questions of 2018 and 2019 are answered by this single object: rows 1–4 give the account, the "why still excluded" block gives the extension answer, and the "honest residue" block gives the verdict.

#### 1. The account of general statements ✅

✅ Universal empirical laws are **synthetic** but cannot be conclusively verified by finitely many observations. ✅ Ayer's weak route treats **relevant observations and successful consequences** as rendering them **probable**. ✅ Later confirmation theory develops **degrees of support**.

⚠️ The argument in one line: a law joins a **system** of hypotheses to observation sentences, and successful predictions support the system without deductively proving the universal.

❌ **The fence.** Potential falsification is **Popper's** contrasting demarcation proposal. It is not Ayer's definition of meaning, and a script that answers the 2019 part with "the positivists said laws are falsifiable" has attributed the wrong doctrine to the wrong man.

#### 2. Can the same account be applied to metaphysical statements? — the 2019 pivot ✅

✅ **No.** A metaphysical statement such as "all reality is spiritual" specifies **no observation that would count for or against it, not even in principle**.

⚠️ The reason must be given at the right level. It is not that metaphysical universals are harder to test, or that we lack instruments. It is that they generate **no prediction under any initial conditions and any auxiliaries**, so the confirmation machinery has nothing to work on. ⚠️ General scientific statements have **systematic empirical consequences** and remain open to confirming and disconfirming evidence; metaphysical universals specify **no comparable test**. The difference is categorical.

❓ **The counter-pressure, stated honestly.** Weakening verification to save science does risk admitting metaphysics, because a sufficiently elaborate auxiliary hypothesis can manufacture relevance (Session 3). The positivist's reply is that **public predictive integration** differs from insulation from evidence: the scientific system's auxiliaries are themselves independently testable, while the metaphysical conjunction's are not. ⚠️ The boundary remains contested, and saying so is more accurate than declaring the reply successful.

#### 3. Can verification account for the meaningfulness of **all** scientific sentences? — the 2018 pivot ✅

⚠️ The disciplined answer has three parts:

1. **Not on the strong sense.** Universal laws, the past, other minds and theoretical entities are all excluded — an unacceptable result.
2. **On the weak sense, yes — but at a price.** Laws are admitted through testable consequences, the past through present traces, other minds through behaviour and explanatory theory, and theoretical terms through their role in a predictive network.
3. **The price is the sharp boundary.** These routes are **indirect** and **theory-dependent**. They weaken simple sentence-by-sentence reduction without making the statements meaningless — which is to say that the criterion survives as an account of **science** while dissolving as a criterion of **meaning**.

- ⚠️ **The one-line verdict:** verification can account for the meaningfulness of scientific sentences only in a form so indirect that it can no longer do the exclusionary work for which it was designed.

#### 4. Theoretical terms and the beginning of holism ⚠️

⚠️ "Electron" has no single corresponding observation. Its significance derives from its role in **laws, auxiliary assumptions and consequences** of a theory-network. ⚠️ The moment that is conceded, the criterion's third presupposition (Session 4, P3) is under strain: if a term draws its content from a network, meaning is not being assigned sentence by sentence.

⚠️ This is the internal route to Duhem–Quine holism, and stating it as **internal** is worth a mark: the positivists' own treatment of theoretical terms generates the pressure that Quine later formalises. ❌ Do not develop "Two Dogmas" here; one clause, and the ownership note — **Topic 11**.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The positivist account of general statements succeeds in saving science, but only by adopting a relation to experience so indirect that the criterion can no longer perform the exclusion for which it was designed.
> **Named evidence.** A universal law such as "all metals expand when heated" quantifies over indefinitely many instances and cannot be conclusively verified by any finite observation set, so Ayer's weak route counts relevant observations and successful consequences as rendering it probable; statements about the past are handled through present records and traces, statements about other minds through publicly observable behaviour and explanatory theory, and theoretical terms such as "electron" through their role in the testable consequences of a theory-network.
> **Analysis.** In every case the law reaches experience only in company — with initial conditions and auxiliary assumptions — so what is confirmed or strained is the system rather than the sentence, which is precisely why the account can be extended to science and cannot be extended to metaphysics: "all reality is spiritual" generates no prediction under any conditions and any auxiliaries, and therefore specifies no comparable test.
> **Qualification.** The gain is a defensible theory of evidential support and the loss is the sharp sentence-by-sentence boundary between the meaningful and the meaningless; and since a theoretical term now draws its content from a network, the positivists' own treatment of science generates the holistic pressure that Quine would later formalise against them.

#### Exam link

- **2019 Q2(a)** (20 marks) is this session's two pivots in order: the account of general statements, then the reasoned refusal to extend it to metaphysical statements.
- **2018 Q3(b)** (15 marks) takes its first half from Session 6 and its second half from §3 here, closing on the "meaningfulness at the cost of the boundary" verdict.
- **2025 Q3(b)** (15 marks) uses row 1 of the visual as the concrete casualty of strong verification, which converts an abstract fork into a demonstrated one.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Positivists said laws are meaningful because they are falsifiable" | Falsifiability is **Popper's** demarcation criterion, not Ayer's account of meaning |
| "Scientific laws are conclusively verified by repeated observation" | No finite set exhausts a universal; the support is **probabilistic and indirect** |
| Answering "can the account be extended to metaphysics?" with a bare "no" | Give the **reason**: no prediction under any conditions and auxiliaries; **no comparable test** |
| "Statements about the past mean statements about present records" | That confuses the **evidence** with the **content**; the sentence is about Caesar, not about the parchment |
| "Other minds reduce to behaviour" | One **behaviourist** route, not the only weak-verification account; do not present it as forced |
| Treating the science/metaphysics difference as one of degree | It is **categorical**: one class has systematic empirical consequences, the other has none |

#### Revision notes

- Universal laws are **synthetic**, not conclusively verifiable; the weak route uses relevant observations and successful consequences.
- Route: law + initial conditions + auxiliaries → prediction → observation → the **system** is confirmed or strained.
- Past: present records and traces. Other minds: behaviour, language, explanatory theory. Theoretical terms: role in a theory-network.
- Costs, one each: support is never proof · evidence must not be confused with content · behaviourism is one route, not forced · network content strains sentence-by-sentence meaning.
- 2019 answer: **no** — metaphysical universals generate no prediction under any conditions; **no comparable test**; the difference is categorical.
- 2018 answer: not on the strong sense; on the weak sense only at the **cost of the sharp boundary**.
- Fence: falsifiability is Popper's **demarcation** criterion (Session 12); holism is **Topic 11**.

```text
   |
```
