**What changed anyway.** ⚠️ Schlick's difference-argument (1932) survives as a heuristic of significance: philosophers who reject the criterion still ask what difference a proposed distinction is supposed to make. ⚠️ The demand for explicit rules rather than obscurity became a professional norm. ⚠️ The agenda of modern philosophy of science and language — confirmation, demarcation, holism, the status of analyticity — is the agenda of positivism's unsolved problems. ⚠️ And the separation of criteria of **meaning**, of **truth** and of **scientific status** is a permanent gain that predates none of the school's critics.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ The assessment is accurate, provided "failed" names four pressures and not one, and provided "changed philosophy" names a surviving method and agenda rather than a surviving doctrine.

#### Original 6 · 20 marks

**Question:** Compare Ayer's and Carnap's versions of the rejection of metaphysics, and assess which is the more defensible. **(Answer in about 350 words.)**

##### Independent model answer

**Thesis.** Ayer's version is a verdict and Carnap's is a reclassification, and the reclassification is the more defensible because it survives objections that the verdict does not.

**Ayer's version.** In *Language, Truth and Logic* (1936) a metaphysical sentence "purports to express a genuine proposition, but does not". It fails both branches of the criterion of cognitive significance and is therefore a pseudo-statement — not false, but without truth-conditions. ⚠️ The strength is clarity and immediate applicability. ⚠️ The weakness is dependence: the verdict is only as secure as the criterion, and the criterion is exposed to the strong/weak fork, to Church's 1949 objection and to self-application.

**Carnap's earlier version.** "The Elimination of Metaphysics through Logical Analysis of Language" (1932) adds what Ayer's verdict lacks — a **diagnosis**. Two routes are distinguished: a term with no empirical criterion of application, and significant terms combined so that no truth-conditions result, the second illustrated by *das Nichts nichtet*, in which a logical quantifier is treated as a name. ⚠️ This explains **how** metaphysical sentences are generated rather than merely condemning them.

**Carnap's developed version.** The principle of tolerance (*Logical Syntax of Language*, 1934/1937, §17) holds that "in logic, there are no morals" and demands syntactical rules instead of philosophical arguments; "Empiricism, Semantics, and Ontology" (1950) then separates **internal** questions, settled by a framework's rules, from **external** questions, which are practical proposals about which language to adopt. ⚠️ Classical ontological disputes are re-described as disguised proposals about vocabulary.

**Assessment.** ⚠️ Three advantages favour Carnap. His version explains why metaphysical disputes are interminable, since proposals have no truth-values. It leaves internal ontology fully legitimate rather than abolishing ontology altogether. And it replaces a controversial criterion of meaning with a checkable demand for explicit syntactical rules. ❓ Its cost is a dependence on the analytic/synthetic boundary that Quine contests, though the Carnapian reply — that pragmatic framework choice need not itself be an analytic truth — remains available.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ Carnap's is the more defensible version: tolerance refines anti-metaphysics rather than repeating it, and Quine pressures, but does not by assertion settle, the boundary on which that refinement depends.

## OPTIONAL ADVANCED DEPTH — NOT REQUIRED FOR A CORE ANSWER

### Prerequisite check before using this block

⚠️ Nothing below is needed to answer any of the **seven** routed 2018–2025 questions. The Basic block already carries the protocol-sentence debate itself, Schlick's foundational reading, Neurath's anti-foundational reply, the resulting collapse of an incorrigible observation base, the physicalist and phenomenalist programmes, the unity-of-science caution, and the migration from verification toward confirmation. This block contains only the **second-order** questions that arise once that account is in place, and each is worth at most **one evaluative sentence** in a fifteen- or twenty-mark answer. ❌ Do not open a paragraph with material from here.

### A1. Was Neurath's coherentism a repair of empiricism, or an abandonment of it? ❓

- **The second-order issue.** Granting the Basic block's narrative, the further question is whether Neurath's move preserved empiricism at all, and whether **intersubjective testability** still deserves the name *verification*.
- **Competing readings.** Schlick- and Carnap-style **foundational** protocol sentences; Neurath's **anti-foundational coherentist** line; later **compromise** positions that keep observation privileged in practice without claiming incorrigibility.
- **Objection.** If observation language is itself theory-laden and revisable, verification loses its bedrock and the criterion loses the epistemic asymmetry it was built on.
- **Counter-reply.** The debate pushed positivists toward **intersubjective testability** rather than infallible experiential atoms — but a coherence-tested system is no longer an *empiricist foundation* in the original sense, which is exactly Quine's later opening.
- **Safe use.** One closing evaluative clause about whether the repair preserved empiricism. ❌ Do not catalogue Vienna Circle memoranda.

### A2. Carnap against Neurath on physicalism and unified science ❓

- **The issue.** Should all meaningful statements be **translated** into a physical language, or merely **coordinated** inside a publicly testable scientific encyclopedia?
- **Competing readings.** Stricter **translational** physicalism; Neurath's **encyclopaedic and coherentist** physicalism; a softer **linguistic-framework** reading on which the choice is pragmatic rather than factual.
- **Objection.** Physicalism appears to exclude mental and cultural vocabulary too quickly.
- **Counter-reply.** Its real aim was **public checkability** and a common language, not a crude denial that mental discourse exists.
- **Safe use.** One extra sentence when assessing whether logical positivism was more nuanced than the slogan "metaphysics is nonsense". ❌ Do not let this become a general philosophy-of-science essay.

### A3. From verification to confirmation — continuity or capitulation? ⚠️

- **The issue.** Did later positivism **preserve** its original meaning-criterion, or **retreat** into a weaker philosophy of scientific support?
- **Competing readings.** A **continuity** reading, on which confirmation theory is the criterion repaired; a **discontinuity** reading, on which confirmation theory is an admission that strict verification failed.
- **Objection.** Confirmation yields no clear criterion of **meaning**, only a theory of evidential support.
- **Counter-reply.** Exactly for that reason it is enrichment rather than Core: it explains the movement's afterlife and why Quine and Popper could attack it so effectively.
- **Safe use.** The last paragraph only, to show historical pressure on the original doctrine. ❌ Do not replace the verification principle with Hempelian confirmation theory in the main answer.

### A4. What the promotion record shows ⚠️

⚠️ The protocol-sentence debate was itself **promoted into Core** because it is required to explain why verification destabilised from within; only the second-order evaluative question in A1 remains optional. ⚠️ Modules A2 and A3 would be promoted only if a future question asked directly about physicalism, the unity of science, intra-positivist disagreement, or how logical positivism changed after its verificationist phase. ⚠️ Stating this ordering is itself a mark of judgement: it shows the candidate knows which optional material has already earned its place in a core answer and which has not.

### Conceptual links — not current-affairs items

⚠️ No dated official or academic development is claimed for this clause. Its live relevance is **disciplinary and static**: the collapse of verificationism set the agenda of contemporary philosophy of science and language — confirmation, demarcation, holism and the status of analyticity — which is why the clause is examined in seven of eight years. That is stated as analysis, not as a news event.

### Source trail for this block

Moritz Schlick · Rudolf Carnap · Otto Neurath · A. J. Ayer, *Language, Truth and Logic* · Carl Hempel · Alonzo Church · Michael Friedman · Thomas Uebel.

## CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

> Compressed revision notes for the complete topic. They cover every subtopic in the Basic block and preserve the Basic / Optional-Advanced distinction; they do not repeat the teaching introductions. Read them after the sessions, not instead of them. The complete ASCII master flow diagram closes the package.

### A. The programme, the Circle, and what is not owned

- **Three printed limbs, one instrument.** *Verification Theory of Meaning* fixes what a sentence must do to say anything; *Rejection of Metaphysics* is what the instrument does to metaphysical sentences; *Linguistic Theory of Necessary Propositions* is what it does to logic and mathematics. Write them in that order.
- **The Circle:** **Schlick** (leader), **Carnap**, **Neurath**, with **Hahn** and **Waismann**; meetings from 1924. **Manifesto 1929**, *Wissenschaftliche Weltauffassung: Der Wiener Kreis* — authors **Hahn, Neurath, Carnap**, **dedicated** to Schlick. It is a **collective** document.
- **Three inheritances, one novelty.** Hume's fork supplies the **content** test; Frege and Russell supply the **form** test; relativity supplies the **model** of fixing content by fixing the method of settling. The novelty is the **combination** — empiricism armed with a formal logic.
- **Philosophy reassigned** (Schlick, "The Turning Point in Philosophy", 1930): an **activity of clarification**, not a system of propositions. Two consequences: there are no philosophical propositions, and the unity of science becomes a **project** rather than a discovery.
- **Internal diversity — three fault lines.** The evidential base (Schlick against Neurath) · the language of science (phenomenalist against physicalist) · the status of the criterion (discovery, proposal or rule). ❌ "All positivists held that…" is almost always false.
- ❌ **Not owned:** falsifiability as doctrine (**Popper** — demarcation, and metaphysics **meaningful**) · holism, ontological relativity and the positive case against analyticity (**Topic 11**) · meaning as use, language-games, private language (**Topic 08**) · the picture theory and saying/showing as doctrine (**Topic 06**) · Hume's epistemology (**Topic 03**) · Kant's transcendental machinery (**Topic 04**) · Hick's eschatological verification (**Paper II**).
- **Corpus signal:** **7 of 112** parts, 2018–2025; **2022** is the only year without an owned part.

### B. Chronology and provenance — examiner-checked

- **1748** Hume, *Enquiry* XII — the fork; "commit it then to the flames". **1921** German *Logisch-Philosophische Abhandlung*; **1922** bilingual *Tractatus*. **1926–27** the Circle reads the *Tractatus* aloud. **1929** manifesto; Heidegger, *Was ist Metaphysik?*. **1929–32** Wittgenstein converses with Schlick and Waismann (McGuinness ed., 1979). **1930** Schlick, "The Turning Point in Philosophy". **1932** Carnap, "Überwindung der Metaphysik…"; Neurath, "Protokollsätze"; Schlick, "Positivism and Realism"; Carnap, "Über Protokollsätze". **1932–33** Ayer attends as a visitor. **1934** Schlick, "Über das Fundament der Erkenntnis"; Carnap, *Logische Syntax der Sprache* (English 1937, §17); Popper, *Logik der Forschung* (English 1959). **1936** Ayer, *Language, Truth and Logic*; Schlick, "Meaning and Verification"; Quine, "Truth by Convention"; **Schlick murdered**, the Circle disperses. **1937** Stevenson, "The Emotive Meaning of Ethical Terms" (*Mind*). **1944** Stevenson, *Ethics and Language*. **1946** *LTL* 2nd edition — the revision is in the **Introduction**. **1949** Church. **1950** Carnap, "Empiricism, Semantics, and Ontology". **1951** Quine, "Two Dogmas of Empiricism". **1960** Quine, *Word and Object* — Neurath's boat as **epigraph**. **1960–65** Geach on embedding.
- ⚠️ **Four date-marks that cost one clause each:** the manifesto's three authors and dedicatee · the 1936/1946 split in Ayer · the 1946/1949 gap between revision and refutation · the non-membership of Wittgenstein, Ayer and Popper, each for a different reason.
- ⚠️ **Provenance flags.** "The meaning of a proposition is the method of its verification" belongs to the **Schlick–Waismann-era** discussions and is **not** a *Tractatus* line. *Konstatierungen* is rendered "affirmations" or "observation statements" — give the German once. Neurath's boat first appears in *Anti-Spengler* (1921) and a 1913 paper; renderings vary, so mark it a rendering. **"Nothing noths"** or **"the nothing nothings"** for *das Nichts nichtet*; ❌ **never "Nothing noughts"**.

### C. The verification criterion — statement, meaning against truth, cognitive significance

- **Statement.** A sentence is **cognitively meaningful if and only if** it is **analytic** — true or false solely in virtue of the meanings of its terms — **or empirically verifiable** — some possible sense-experience is relevant to its truth or falsehood. Failing both, it is a **pseudo-statement (*Scheinsatz*)**: not a fact-stater, and **not even false**.
- **The biconditional has two halves.** Left to right it **licenses** logic, mathematics and science; right to left it **excludes** everything else. An "exposition" must give both.
- **Meaning, not truth.** "The earth is flat" passes the test and is **false**; "the Absolute is beyond time" receives **no truth-value**. ⚠️ Therefore the positivist may **not** add that the Absolute does not exist — a denial needs the truth-conditions he says are missing.
- **In principle, not in practice.** "There are mountains on the far side of the moon" was meaningful before spaceflight, because the settling observations could be **specified**. "Beyond all experience" fails even in principle, because the sentence forbids the specification.
- **"Cognitive" is not a hedge.** It restricts the verdict to **fact-stating capacity**. Commands, questions, promises, jokes, ritual formulas, expressions of feeling and poetry are untouched **as uses**.
- **Verdict vocabulary:** *pseudo-statement* for the category; *cognitively meaningless* for the verdict. ❌ Never "false"; ❌ never "unprovable"; ⚠️ "nonsense" only with the technical gloss attached.
- ✅ **Ayer:** a metaphysical sentence "purports to express a genuine proposition, but does not."

### D. Strong and weak verifiability, the revision and Church

- **Strong:** truth can be **conclusively established** by a **finite** set of observations. Both words are load-bearing.
- **Weak:** some possible observations are **relevant** — would render it **probable or improbable**.
- **Strong's casualties:** universal laws · statements about the past · statements about other minds. **Verdict: strong verification kills science.** Only singular present-tense reports survive.
- **Weak's casualty:** the exclusion itself. Relevance is cheap: an auxiliary hypothesis yields an observation-statement from the pair that does not follow from the auxiliary alone.
- **The repair:** *LTL* 1936 uses the weak sense; the **1946 Introduction** substitutes **direct** verifiability (alone, or with observation-statements, entails an observation-statement not deducible from those premisses alone) and **indirect** verifiability (with further premisses that are analytic, directly verifiable, or independently indirectly verifiable, entails a directly verifiable statement not deducible from those premisses alone).
- **The refutation: Church, 1949** — the revised criterion admits statements it was designed to exclude; given three mutually independent observation-statements, an arbitrary sentence satisfies the conditions.
- **After 1949:** verification → **confirmation and testability**. Gain: a better account of evidential support. Loss: the sharp sentence-by-sentence boundary.
- ⚠️ **Attribution:** strong/weak is **Ayer's** vocabulary. ⚠️ The fork is the principle's **internal** problem and is **distinct** from self-refutation.

### E. The positive case, the argument and its four presuppositions

- **Five motivations, graded.** (1) The **difference-argument** (Schlick, "Positivism and Realism", 1932) — hypotheses agreeing on every possible observation are one hypothesis in two vocabularies; a principle of **significance**, and the one that **survives**. (2) The **Einstein model** — operational attention to how distant simultaneity is fixed; an influence, not a proof that meaning **is** one measurement procedure. (3) **Understanding and use-conditions** — bounded; ❌ not later meaning-as-use. (4) The **demarcation motive** — Popper's rival answers a different question. (5) **Unity of science** — internally disputed.
- **The six steps.** Understand → know truth-conditions → discriminate the circumstances → know what counts as evidence → understanding **is** grasping verification-conditions → a sentence with no possible evidence has **nothing for understanding to consist in** → it is **not false** but without cognitive content.
- **The four presuppositions, and the objection each generates.** **P1** truth-conditions reduce to observation-conditions — never argued for. **P2** observation-statements are theory-neutral — broken **from within** by the protocol debate. **P3** sentences face experience **individually** — Duhem–Quine holism, and already strained by the positivists' own theory-network account of theoretical terms. **P4** the analytic/synthetic line is sharp — "Two Dogmas".
- ⚠️ **Why this earns twenty-mark space:** it converts a list of objections into a **structure**, since each major criticism is the denial of one hinge.

### F. The *Tractatus* lineage — taken, added, three fences

- **Taken:** the boundary project · logic as **tautology** · philosophy as an **activity** · the exposure of surface grammar.
- **Added:** **truth-conditions → verification-conditions** — a **logical** requirement replaced by an **epistemic** one.
- **Why the substitution is not a detail.** Universal laws have specifiable **truth-conditions** but no finite conclusive **verification-conditions**, so the substitution itself generates the strong/weak fork. And the base changes category: Tractarian objects are **postulated** by a transcendental argument, positivist protocols are **claimed to be found** — which is why a protocol **debate** was possible at all.
- **Three fences.** (i) Wittgenstein was **never a member**. (ii) The *Tractatus* is **not** a verificationist text; its criterion of sense is **depiction**. (iii) **Later** Wittgenstein endorses **no** single criterion of meaning — Topic 08.
- **Dated contact:** German 1921 · bilingual 1922 · Circle reading 1926–27 · conversations 1929–32 · McGuinness volume 1979.

### G. The rejection of metaphysics — procedure, routes, examples

- **Procedure.** Step 1: analytic? → meaningful and empty, stop. Step 2: any possible observation relevant, in principle? → meaningful and synthetic, stop. Step 3: **diagnose the route**. Step 4: verdict **pseudo-statement**, plus a record of what is **not** claimed.
- **Route 1 — no empirical criterion of application:** "the Absolute", "Being-in-itself", "the noumenon", "the soul".
- **Route 2 — illicit combination:** "Caesar is a prime number"; and *das Nichts nichtet*, the paradigm, which treats a **logical quantifier as a name** and predicates a **pseudo-verb** of it (Heidegger 1929; Carnap's analysis, *Erkenntnis*, 1932).
- **Controls — include one.** "Electrons have negative charge" is **meaningful**, though testable only indirectly; "2 + 2 = 4" is **meaningful** and analytic. Without a control the test reads as a blacklist.
- **Heidegger's available reply:** *das Nichts* is disclosed in the mood of anxiety (*Angst*), and the breach of logical grammar is **deliberate**, since logic presupposes the prior disclosure of beings as a whole. ⚠️ Report it, then rule.
- **The cascade (2023).** **Meaning** is the root: no truth-conditions. **Knowledge** is **dissolved**, not answered — one can neither know nor fail to know a non-proposition; this is stronger and stranger than agnosticism. **Ontology**: the **question** is ill-formed; ❌ never "these things do not exist". **Verdict: all three, through meaning as the root.**
- **Two refinements that prevent a slogan.** Carnap's framework analysis (part K) is a stronger anti-metaphysics than Ayer's verdict; and the ordinary-language objection denies that one factual-test model exhausts significance without thereby vindicating metaphysics.

### H. Ethics, aesthetics and theology

- **Why ethics is here:** Ayer **applies** the criterion to value language, so emotivism is a **corollary**; and the 2020 part asks about "Good".
- **Ayer** (*LTL* 1936, ch. 6): the value term is a **pure expression** — it **evinces** disapproval and may **evoke** it, adding **no factual content**; ethical disputes are really about facts, and once facts are agreed "we should not attempt to argue".
- **Stevenson** (*Mind* 1937; *Ethics and Language* 1944): ethical terms have **descriptive** and **emotive** meaning; "X is good" ≈ "I approve of X; do so as well"; **disagreement in belief** against **disagreement in attitude**, the latter surviving complete factual agreement; **rational** against **persuasive** methods; **persuasive definition**.
- ⚠️ **Both are non-cognitivists, not subjectivists.** Neither treats "X is wrong" as a **report** of feeling — that would make moral disagreement autobiographical. Getting this right is worth a mark by itself.
- **Frege–Geach embedding problem** (Geach 1960, 1965; anticipated by Frege): the unasserted antecedent of "if lying is wrong, then getting your brother to lie is wrong" expresses no attitude yet must retain its meaning. **Decisive** against simple emotivism; Blackburn and Gibbard later attempt logics of attitude; neither Ayer nor Stevenson had an answer.
- **Theology at owned depth:** transcendent existence-claims fail by route 1; ⚠️ this is **not atheism**, which would be a meaningful denial; ritual and expressive uses are a separate question; **Hick's eschatological verification** is one clause and belongs to **Paper II**.
- **The 2020 sentence, split:** subject-term with **no criterion of application** (fictional bearer) **plus** non-descriptive **value predicate** → cognitively meaningless, **not false**; and within an explicitly stated fiction-framework a claim about the text would be checkable.

### I. The linguistic theory of necessary propositions

- **The trilemma:** necessary **+** a priori **+** certain, against "all knowledge comes from experience".
- **Three rivals.** **Kant** — synthetic a priori; empiricists reject a third box. **Mill** — empirical generalisation; refuted because we do not discover "2 + 2 = 4" by counting and **no experience could refute it**, an apparent counter-instance being put down to miscounting. **Positivism** — **analytic**: true by the meanings and conventions governing the symbols; **tautologies**; **no information about the world**.
- **Four features from one source.** Necessary — nothing could count against them. A priori — grasping the meanings suffices. Certain — denial violates one's own conventions. **Empty** — the price, and the point.
- **The exhaustive dichotomy.** Analytic (necessary, a priori, empty, certain) | synthetic (contingent, a posteriori, factual, fallible). **No third box** → the **synthetic a priori is declared incoherent**; Kant appears here **only as the target**.
- **The 2024 pair.** "All objects are either red or not red" — analytic, form **P ∨ ¬P**, true under all truth-conditions, refutable by no observation **because empty**. "This page is white" — synthetic, checkable, **could be false**. **Answer: no — both meaningful, by different branches**; metaphysics fits neither.
- **Pressures, named honestly.** Quine, "Truth by Convention" (1936) — applying conventions to derive their consequences already presupposes logic. Quine, "Two Dogmas" (1951) — analyticity is not non-circularly definable. **Applicability** — if mathematics is empty, why indispensable? Reply: applicability belongs to the **embedding empirical theory**. **Gödel** — constrains particular formal systems; ❌ not a one-line refutation of conventionalism.

### J. General statements, hard cases and confirmation

- **The problem:** a universal law quantifies over indefinitely many instances; no finite observation set exhausts it.
- **The route:** **law + initial conditions + auxiliaries → prediction → observation report → the system is confirmed or strained.** Support, never proof; later confirmation theory supplies **degrees**.
- **Four hard cases with their costs.** Universal laws — failure implicates the **system**, not one sentence. The **past** — present records and traces; ⚠️ do not confuse the **evidence** with the **content**, since the sentence is about Caesar, not the parchment. **Other minds** — behaviour, language and explanatory theory; ⚠️ behaviourist reduction is **one route**, not forced. **Theoretical terms** — content from a **theory-network**; ⚠️ which concedes that meaning is not assigned sentence by sentence.
- **The 2019 extension test.** "All reality is spiritual" generates **no prediction under any conditions and any auxiliaries**, so the machinery has nothing to work on. Scientific universals have **systematic empirical consequences**; metaphysical universals specify **no comparable test**. The difference is **categorical**.
- ❓ **Counter-pressure, conceded:** weakening verification does risk admitting metaphysics; the reply is that **public predictive integration** differs from insulation from evidence, since scientific auxiliaries are themselves independently testable. The boundary remains contested.
- **The 2018 verdict:** not on the strong sense; on the weak sense **only at the cost of the sharp boundary**.
- ❌ **Fence:** potential falsification is **Popper's demarcation** proposal, not Ayer's definition of meaning.

### K. Protocol sentences, tolerance and the unity of science

- **The question:** what is the form and status of **protocol sentences (*Protokollsätze*)** — incorrigible foundations, or ordinary revisable sentences?
- **Schlick, 1934:** **affirmations (*Konstatierungen*)**, "here now blue", **absolutely certain** because understanding and verifying are **one act**; the "unshakeable point of contact". **Catch:** an affirmation **cannot be written down and preserved** — recording it yields a hypothesis about a past moment; the certainty is **momentary and unusable**.
- **Neurath, 1932:** **physicalist**, observer- and time-indexed protocols, **fallible and revisable**; "statements are compared with statements, not with 'experiences'"; **the boat** — sailors rebuilding their ship on the open sea.
- **Carnap, 1932:** the **form** of a protocol is a **matter of choice of language** — tolerance applied to the Circle's own foundations, which **ends foundationalism inside the Circle**.
- **The residue.** Schlick: coherence alone admits a **consistent fairy-tale**. Neurath: selection is made by **encyclopaedic scientific practice**. ⚠️ That makes the choice **sociological rather than epistemic**; the charge is **not fully met**.
- **Casualty:** presupposition **P2**. **Effect:** movement toward intersubjective, system-level testing. **Aftermath:** Quine's boat epigraph (*Word and Object*, 1960) is an acknowledged **affinity**, not a derivation.
- **Tolerance** (*Logical Syntax*, 1934/1937, **§17**): "In logic, there are no morals… give **syntactical rules instead of philosophical arguments**." Four steps: no uniquely correct language · framework choice is **pragmatic** · classical ontological disputes are **disguised proposals** · logic-choice is a choice of instrument.
- **Internal/external** (1950): "Is there a prime number greater than 100?" is **internal**, settled by the rules; "Are there **really** numbers?" is **external** — **not a theoretical question**, but the practical question whether to adopt the number-language. ⚠️ Internal ontology therefore remains **legitimate**.
- **Three programmes, three cautions.** Phenomenalist reconstruction — private-language and other-minds risk. Physicalist public language — Carnap and Neurath **differ**; not reduction to private sensation. Unity of science — **translation, coordination or method**, ❌ not one settled reduction theorem.

### L. Exam craft — directives, architecture, traps and the answer spine

- **Rank the four pressures.** (1) The **strong/weak fork** — internal; Church 1949; no formulation ever worked. (2) The **protocol debate** — internal; a **chosen** base cannot ground a **discovered** criterion. (3) **Analyticity and holism** — Quine 1951, Topic 11. (4) **Self-refutation** — the criterion is neither analytic nor verifiable.
- **Self-refutation in two sentences, with costs.** Reply: a **recommendation** → cost: a policy may be **declined**. Reply: **analytic of** the proposed concept → cost: **circular**. Reply: a **meta-linguistic rule** → cost: the question **recurs**. Residue: the reply avoids direct contradiction but weakens a discovery into a **recommended linguistic policy**.
- **Contrasts, not repairs.** Popper — **demarcation**, and metaphysics **meaningful**. Later Wittgenstein — **no** single criterion, Topic 08. Ordinary language — commands, avowals, performatives, jokes, mathematics and religious practices may not be **failed empirical reports**.
- **Directive decoder.** "Discuss the verification theory" → positive case **first**. "Critically examine the rejection" → the **diagnosis** is the marked part. "Elucidate the linguistic theory" → state the **trilemma** first. "Differentiate strong and weak" → the **dilemma**, not two definitions. "How does one identify pseudostatements" → a **procedure** plus a **control case**. "Comment on 'the nothing nothings'" → attribute correctly, analyse the **grammatical** error, then report Heidegger's reply. "Assess emotivism" → **split Ayer from Stevenson** and rule. "Trace Wittgenstein's influence" → what was **taken** and what was **added**.
- **Answer spine.** **10 marks:** criterion → one false-but-meaningful illustration → verdict word → one qualification. **15 marks:** frame (meaning **not** truth) → both halves of the biconditional → the doctrine asked for → one named objection with its reply → graded verdict. **20 marks:** frame → full doctrine → the hard cases → **two** of the four pressures, ranked, at least one **internal** → the doctrine's own **development** (Carnap after Ayer) → qualified conclusion.
- **Ten traps in one line each.** Criterion of truth ❌ · meaningless = false ❌ · "metaphysical objects do not exist" ❌ · unverifiable in practice = meaningless ❌ · Schlick wrote the manifesto ❌ · Ayer was a member ❌ · Popper repaired verificationism ❌ · the *Tractatus* founded positivism ❌ · "Nothing noughts" ❌ · emotivism = subjectivism ❌.
- **Memory chain:** the fork and the new logic → the Circle and the manifesto → philosophy as clarification → the two-key criterion → meaning against truth → strong and weak → the positive case and its four hinges → the *Tractatus* substitution → the pseudo-statement diagnostic → value and theology → necessity made verbal → laws, hard cases and confirmation → the missing floor → frameworks and tolerance → the ranked criticism ledger.
- **Qualified conclusion.** ⚠️ Logical positivism proposed a single test to separate what can be said from what only looks as though it were said, and the test failed in four different ways — two of them discovered by positivists themselves, in the strong/weak fork and in the protocol dispute. What survives is not the doctrine but a discipline: Schlick's difference-argument as a heuristic of significance, the demand for explicit rules rather than obscurity, the permanent separation of criteria of **meaning**, of **truth** and of **scientific status**, and an agenda — confirmation, demarcation, holism, analyticity — that is simply the list of problems the Circle could not solve. An answer that states the achievement and its matching cost in the same sentence has understood the clause; an answer that ends with self-refutation alone has recalled its most famous line.

     cognitively meaningless        no truth-conditions, hence no truth-value
     'false'                        WRONG - falsity presupposes truth-conditions
     'unprovable'                   WRONG - this concedes it is a proposition
     'nonsense' unqualified         RISKY - invites the ordinary-language reading

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 10/30: THE STRONG SENSE AND ITS CASUALTIES
==============================================================================
   STRONG  a statement is verifiable iff its truth can be CONCLUSIVELY ESTABLISHED with
   certainty by a FINITE set of observations.   Both words are load-bearing.
   ADVANTAGE  rigorous; excludes metaphysics cleanly
   CASUALTIES
     UNIVERSAL LAWS   'All metals expand when heated' - indefinitely many instances; no
                      finite set exhausts them
     THE PAST         'Caesar crossed the Rubicon' - not now observable
     OTHER MINDS      'She is in pain' - another's experience is not my observation
   SURVIVES  singular present-tense observation reports: 'this page is white'
                                     |
                                     v
   VERDICT  STRONG VERIFICATION KILLS SCIENCE. A criterion of meaning that convicts the
   sentences of physics is self-evidently the wrong criterion, and Ayer says so.
   TRAP  naming only universal laws gives ONE THIRD of the casualty list and weakens the
   dilemma the answer is trying to establish.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 11/30: THE WEAK SENSE AND ITS COST
==============================================================================
   WEAK  a statement is verifiable iff some possible observations are RELEVANT to its truth
   or falsehood - would render it PROBABLE or IMPROBABLE.
   ADVANTAGE  saves scientific generalisations and statements about the past and the future
   COST       RELEVANCE IS CHEAP. Conjoin the metaphysical sentence with an AUXILIARY
              HYPOTHESIS and an observation-statement follows from the PAIR that does not
              follow from the auxiliary ALONE -> metaphysics creeps back in
                                     |
                                     v
   THE FORK, STATED ONCE
   Tight enough to exclude metaphysics is tight enough to exclude physics.
   Loose enough to admit physics is loose enough to admit metaphysics.
   NO SETTING OF THE DIAL DOES BOTH JOBS.
   THIS IS THE PRINCIPLE'S INTERNAL PROBLEM. It needs no external critic and is DISTINCT
   from the self-refutation objection at [29]. Keeping them apart is itself a mark.
   ATTRIBUTION FENCE  STRONG/WEAK is AYER'S vocabulary. Do NOT write 'the Vienna Circle
   distinguished strong and weak verification' as one agreed Circle doctrine.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/30: THE REPAIR AND ITS FAILURE - THREE DATES, NEVER MERGED
==============================================================================
   1936  LTL 1st ed.   WEAK test: observations that make the proposition PROBABLE
   1946  LTL 2nd ed.   the INTRODUCTION supplies a technical replacement:
         DIRECTLY VERIFIABLE    it is itself an observation-statement, OR with one or more
                                observation-statements it entails an observation-statement
                                NOT deducible from those premisses alone
         INDIRECTLY VERIFIABLE  with further premisses it entails a DIRECTLY verifiable
                                statement not deducible from those premisses alone, AND
                                those premisses are analytic, or directly verifiable, or
                                independently shown to be indirectly verifiable
   1949  CHURCH        the revised criterion ADMITS statements it was designed to EXCLUDE:
                       given three mutually independent observation-statements, an
                       ARBITRARY sentence can be shown to satisfy the conditions
                                     |
                                     v
   MIGRATION  VERIFICATION -> CONFIRMATION and TESTABILITY of SYSTEMS
   GAIN  a far better account of how evidence supports scientific theory
   LOSS  the sharp, sentence-by-sentence boundary between meaningful and meaningless

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 14/30: THE ARGUMENT IN SIX STEPS
==============================================================================
   1  To UNDERSTAND a sentence is to know what it would be for it to be TRUE
                                     |
   2  To know that is to DISTINGUISH the circumstances in which it holds from those in
      which it does not
                                     |
   3  To distinguish those circumstances is to know what would count as EVIDENCE either way
                                     |
   4  .:  UNDERSTANDING a sentence = GRASPING ITS VERIFICATION-CONDITIONS
                                     |
   5  .:  a sentence for which NO evidence could count either way has NOTHING for
      understanding to consist in
                                     |
   6  .:  such a sentence is NOT FALSE; it is WITHOUT COGNITIVE CONTENT

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 17/30: REJECTION OF METAPHYSICS - THE IDENTIFICATION PROCEDURE
==============================================================================
   STEP 1  Is the sentence ANALYTIC - true or false by meanings and rules alone?
              YES -> MEANINGFUL and factually EMPTY.   STOP
              NO  -> Step 2
   STEP 2  Can ANY possible observation count FOR or AGAINST it (in principle)?
              YES -> MEANINGFUL, synthetic.            STOP
              NO  -> Step 3
   STEP 3  DIAGNOSE the failure - WHICH ROUTE?
              ROUTE 1  a constituent TERM has NO EMPIRICAL CRITERION OF APPLICATION
              ROUTE 2  individually significant terms are COMBINED so that NO
                       TRUTH-CONDITIONS RESULT
   STEP 4  VERDICT  PSEUDO-STATEMENT (Scheinsatz).
              Record what is NOT claimed: not false; not unproven; not unintelligible;
              expressive and practical force untouched
   SOURCE  CARNAP, 'Uberwindung der Metaphysik durch logische Analyse der Sprache',
   Erkenntnis, 1932.  AYER: a metaphysical sentence 'purports to express a genuine
   proposition, but does not.'
   2021 Q2(b) ASKS FOR THIS PROCEDURE, not for a list of condemned sentences.

   COUNTER-PRESSURE, conceded  weakening verification DOES risk admitting metaphysics; the

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 28/30: TOLERANCE, FRAMEWORKS, REDUCTION AND THE UNITY OF SCIENCE
==============================================================================
   PRINCIPLE OF TOLERANCE  Logische Syntax der Sprache, 1934; English 1937, s.17
   'IN LOGIC, THERE ARE NO MORALS. Everyone is at liberty to build up his own logic, i.e.
   his own form of language, as he wishes. All that is required of him is that, if he wishes
   to discuss it, he must state his methods clearly, and give SYNTACTICAL RULES INSTEAD OF
   PHILOSOPHICAL ARGUMENTS.'
     1  NO single 'correct' logic or language is GIVEN by reality - only FRAMEWORKS
     2  a framework is chosen on PRAGMATIC grounds - fruitfulness, simplicity, convenience -
        NOT on grounds of truth
     3  .:  traditional metaphysical disputes are DISGUISED PROPOSALS about which framework
        to adopt
     4  .:  intuitionist vs classical logic, phenomenalist vs physicalist language, is a
        CHOICE OF INSTRUMENT, not a factual disagreement
   INTERNAL / EXTERNAL  'Empiricism, Semantics, and Ontology', 1950
     INTERNAL  'Is there a prime number greater than 100?' asked WITHIN the arithmetical
               framework -> settled by MATHEMATICAL RULES AND PROOF. YES
     EXTERNAL  'Are there REALLY numbers?' asked ABOUT the framework as a whole -> NOT A
               THEORETICAL QUESTION AT ALL. It is the PRACTICAL question whether to adopt
               the number-language, answered by USEFULNESS
   CONSEQUENCE  internal ontological questions remain LEGITIMATE; Carnap therefore DOES NOT
   ABOLISH EVERY USE OF ONTOLOGY. This is a STRONGER anti-metaphysics than Ayer's verdict
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   OBSERVATION LANGUAGE, REDUCTION AND UNITY - three programmes, three cautions
   PHENOMENALIST /      object talk is RECONSTRUCTED from elementary EXPERIENTIAL statements
   RECONSTRUCTIONIST    CAUTION  risks private-language and other-minds problems
   PHYSICALIST /        protocols and scientific claims use an INTERSUBJECTIVELY TESTABLE
   PUBLIC-LANGUAGE      physical language
                        CAUTION  Neurath and Carnap versions DIFFER; NOT simple reduction
                        to private sensation
   UNITY OF SCIENCE     the sciences coordinated in a COMMON LANGUAGE or ENCYCLOPEDIA
                        CAUTION  'unity' may mean TRANSLATION, COORDINATION or
                        METHODOLOGICAL INTEGRATION - NOT one settled reduction theorem
   TRAP  'the logical positivists held that all sciences reduce to physics' attributes to
   the school a thesis its two principal figures DISPUTED
   QUINE'S BOUNDED ATTACK  the internal/external distinction depends on an analytic/
   synthetic boundary he rejects; he favours ontology CONTINUOUS with total theory
   CARNAPIAN REPLY  pragmatic framework choice need NOT be a disputed analytic truth
   VERDICT  tolerance REFINES anti-metaphysics; Quine PRESSURES but does not by assertion
   SETTLE the boundary on which that refinement depends                       TOPIC 11

   ANSWER SPINE
   10 MARKS  state the criterion -> one FALSE-BUT-MEANINGFUL illustration -> the verdict
             word -> ONE qualification
   15 MARKS  frame (meaning NOT truth) -> exposition of BOTH halves of the biconditional ->
             the specific doctrine asked for -> ONE named objection with its reply ->
             graded verdict
   20 MARKS  frame -> full doctrine -> the hard cases -> at least TWO of the four pressures,
             ranked and with at least one INTERNAL -> the doctrine's own DEVELOPMENT
             (Carnap after Ayer) -> qualified conclusion
   DIRECTIVES  'exposition' demands BOTH halves - 'differentiate' demands the DILEMMA, not
   two definitions - 'how does one identify' demands a PROCEDURE - 'critically' demands the
   opponent's REPLY reported before the ruling - 'all of them together' demands an ORDERING
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   QUALIFIED CONCLUSION
   Logical positivism proposed a single test to separate what can be said from what only
   looks as if it were said, and the test failed in four different ways - two of them
   discovered by positivists themselves. It survives not as a doctrine but as a discipline:
   the difference-argument as a heuristic of significance, the demand for explicit rules
   rather than obscurity, the permanent separation of criteria of MEANING, of TRUTH and of
   SCIENTIFIC STATUS, and an agenda - confirmation, demarcation, holism, analyticity - that
   is simply the list of problems the Circle could not solve.
   STATE THE ACHIEVEMENT AND ITS MATCHING COST IN THE SAME SENTENCE.
